# Stories of useful work

**Owner: O:I #65. Standing: source-grounded intended-experience elaboration under the user's campaign commission; specified, not executed or human-validated.** Read the common source/authority rules in [README](README.md), the compatible record form in [STORY-PROFILE](STORY-PROFILE.md), and P01–P18 in [PRACTICE-CONDITIONS](PRACTICE-CONDITIONS.md). P references supply shared practice obligations; each row supplies their concrete determining conditions. They do not stand for installed Skills.

These are child stories grouped by actual activities, not product screens. Each has its own starting material, human act, agent work, observable result and failure/re-entry. At execution bind actual source/capability/Skill IDs and evidence to the row rather than changing its meaning to match the implementation. Intended outcome is not a script of clicks or an expected sentence to teach the tested agent. The full exact document/QL sources outrank the compact examples here.

The stable ID is a row locator and may become the existing UX `id`; the family is its `parent_ref`. All families map to #65 A–I and existing domain acceptance in the campaign manifest. Context omitted from a compact row inherits the explicitly scoped common packet, never ambient private access.

## AD — Add only what helps my existing setup

Sources: founding positions §§2–4; O:I #93/#173/#192, INSTALL; current bootstrap/loading specification. #65 A/D/F/H. Exercise setup context, selected products and entry surface as separate axes; do not equate the six installer entry labels with formal CF1–CF7 or force a desktop.

| ID | Person's starting situation and action | Agent work and determining conditions | Useful completion | Failure, refusal or continuation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AD01 | I have a repository, native agent instructions and an already configured coding tool. Ask to connect the useful parts without moving them. | P01/P02/P07: inspect permitted native files and installed target edition; distinguish their source from generated copies; preview exact reversible additions. | The same ordinary tool performs a task using the intended existing instructions; originals and custom extensions remain intact. When I paste the adoption request into a conversation I already have, the agent first says what it found and what each addition would do, then acts on what I accept; afterwards it names the harness and model that did the first task, the practice they received, what changed and where it lies. | Cancel setup or repeat it; no partial instruction overwrite, duplicate links, forced relocation or second configuration truth. An unknown tool or a conflicting configuration is named as unsupported and kept as it is, while the supported task still works. A conversation that cannot run commands here names the exact step for me to take and never claims my computer changed. A missing part of the bootstrap is named, never replaced by an older release or a hand-copied binary. |
| AD02 | Start with no personal ground. Choose a place for ordinary files and decline agent setup for now. | P01/P05: establish only selected source structure via native owner; leave private preferences unwritten and optional products absent. | The person can find/open/edit their files, then add agency later without migrating them again. | Empty personal fields are valid; absent credentials/Factory/desktop cannot make this chosen minimal setup invalid. Cancelling midway and running setup again keeps what was established, registers nothing twice and never reports an unfinished step as done. |
| AD03 | I already have Central material and local customisations, but a newer installation expects different structure. Ask for reconciliation. | P01/P04/P13/P17: inspect actual roles, old sources, pending work and links; stage a reversible plan and test it before authorised application. | Existing documents, agent knowledge, histories and active tasks remain addressable after reconciliation. | Interrupt/repeat migration; retain dirty files and exact before-state; no deletion merely because a folder is not registered. A generated copy the person edited keeps both versions and gets a proposed reconciliation. An agent a tool already defines is recognised by who it is, never made again because its name matches. |
| AD04 | Add a second supported coding harness while keeping the first and its native customisations. | P02/P06/P07: resolve actual edition capabilities, source/trust/scope and supported loading; preserve foreign settings. | A fresh second-tool session follows the same selected project practice, while both tools keep their own legitimate differences. The agent tells me what is active now and what waits for a reload, a new session or a restart, which session that affects and how to carry on. | Unsupported hooks or loading are explained before promising parity; remove integration without deleting user-installed extensions. A missing link or descriptor, or a body that was not reloaded, names the stage that failed and uses only the approved repair; configured is never reported as loaded. My tool is never restarted to make a check pass. |
| AD05 | Ask for better instructions and knowledge in the terminal; explicitly decline Factory and the desktop. | P01/P03/P07: resolve only the selected source/context/practice capabilities and native entry; no invisible full-suite expansion. | Ordinary tasks use relevant guidance and prior knowledge without opening O:I UI or generating Factory work. | Missing optional products stay absent; later enrichment does not replace project or Agent identity. A missing required model or tool is explained as the exact setup choice; nothing unrelated is installed to cover it. |
| AD06 | Prepare a second workstation using the reference host arrangement and its own desktop/terminal tools. | P01/P14/P17: read machine intent and actual OS/services; preview native host integration and retain required manual OS permission only where genuine. | Open the intended workspace and run a real task with the configured tools; the machine's own settings remain inspectable. The integration also works through the intended remote route. | Failed host reload leaves other work usable; unsupported reference capabilities are not faked by successful file copying. A configuration conflict, an interrupted install or an unsupported OS gets a reversible, scoped response. |
| AD07 | An existing tool/version is not recognised during setup. Ask whether support can be added. | P02/P15: inspect its actual native interface, find the owner SDK and conformance route; stage scoped extension work under permission. | After actual implementation/conformance/register, discovery improves and the intended task uses the newly supported operation. | No conformance-only claim that the human task works; unsupported behavior is preserved as a named limitation, not guessed. |
| AD08 | Inspect the proposed setup and choose a smaller arrangement or defer a requested permission. | P01/P10: show what this choice enables/disables and why using native composition evidence; separate formal CF reading from install authority. | The chosen partial setup performs its promised use; the person can later add the declined capability. Adding the deeper capability later leaves the earlier simple work working, and removing it again keeps the person's source. | No mandatory wizard step for an unchosen feature; declining does not silently install/reconfigure it anyway. A composition recipe that is not yet resolved stays named with its owner; no package list is derived from the CF numerals. |
| AD09 | Remove or switch an O:I integration after ordinary use, keeping files and native tools. | P05/P07/P13/P17: inspect managed ownership, local edits, outstanding work and supported rollback/retraction before removing. | Personal source, knowledge and outputs remain accessible; native tool behavior is restored or its deliberate remaining changes explained. | Do not erase data or remove foreign configuration; retain unresolved lifecycle effects rather than reporting a falsely clean uninstall. Active work is brought to a deliberate stop or handed on, never killed wholesale; a shared file edited since setup is kept. |

## WK — Work normally without supervising the machinery

Sources: O:I #93, TUI §§1/4/7/9, Central source protocol, CAW P07–P10/P16/P17/P21/P22. Ordinary native tools are primary entries, not inferior fallbacks.

| ID | Person's starting situation and action | Agent work and determining conditions | Useful completion | Failure, refusal or continuation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| WK01 | Open the usual coding tool in an existing repo and ask to fix a real defect. | P01/P02/P03/P04/P10: discover project intent and tests through normal setup, select the right working copy, make/check the authorised change. | Actual changed code and executed relevant checks; no need to paste project conventions or open desktop/Factory. | Remove a required instruction/tool in a controlled trial; the agent identifies the missing step rather than inventing success. |
| WK02 | Reopen the research collection after a gap and ask what remains unresolved. | P01/P03/P13: retrieve governing notes/current sources and outstanding questions, not merely last chat text; disclose uncertain history. | A useful answer points to the actual unfinished questions and source versions; the person can continue directly. | Unavailable archive/source produces a specific gap; the agent does not fabricate a past decision. Completed steps are not repeated, and a source that changed since is named and the work reoriented before any effect. |
| WK03 | Edit a source in an ordinary external editor while O:I is not open, then ask a relevant question in the normal harness. | P03/P12: reconcile actual revision, find affected knowledge, select current source rather than silently trust an old summary. | The later answer reflects or explicitly flags the changed basis without the person asking to sync everything. | Mere source change/open/index refresh produces no unrequested model call; stale knowledge is not falsely declared false or fresh. |
| WK04 | Keep unrelated native agent processes alongside an O:I-started conversation and a Factory task. | P06/P10/P14: correlate only actual bindings; distinguish observed external process, known session and commissioned work. | The person can inspect what is really running without unrelated work acquiring Factory ownership. | Kill one process; show its actual loss without marking every task failed or inferring Agent identity solely from PID. |
| WK05 | Stop looking at a long task, work in another application, then ask for its result. | P08/P10/P13: retain exact task/source/receiving route and real activity independent of UI attachment. | A concise account names the actual output, checks, remaining issue and optional evidence; it is available through the chosen supported receiving route. | No requirement to watch logs; closing a pane is not cancel; duplicate delivery does not create duplicate results. |
| WK06 | Ask for help revising an ordinary document in the editor already in use, without opening O:I. | P01/P03/P05: resolve only the relevant style, source and context and the permitted editing route for that editor. | Useful proposed or authorised edits; the unrelated text is kept and the document is not turned into a Day or a Flow. | Where the editor has no supported integration, the agent offers the route that does exist and never claims an edit happened inside the editor. |
| WK07 | I tell an agent, in plain words, that a task did not go so well. | P01/P03/P10/P11: keep my words as my response, not a verified defect; find the task, its NOW, its originating purpose and expected result; propose the smallest plausible scope and show it before looking wider; set what was meant beside what came back and tell apart an unsuccessful result, too much cost or repetition, an unwanted interruption, missing context, a machine or provider failure and a test that expected the wrong thing. | I see what was meant and what came back, with the work's own evidence, refine what mattered, and get a next step with its owner: keep the observation, test it, change a practice, propose a clarification or commission a repair. | The original record is not rewritten, and the failed occasion stays reachable after any repair. An unclear "this" never opens my whole private world; looking wider is my explicit choice. Not every concern becomes a Factory run. |

## GV — Write and maintain the instructions that matter

Sources: Central CONTROL-CONTENT-PROTOCOL §§2–5/10–15, #72/#164, AIKit source/context/Skill practice; CAW P01/P04/P05/P07/P25/P28. Root/meta-Project and child scopes must both work.

| ID | Person's starting situation and action | Agent work and determining conditions | Useful completion | Failure, refusal or continuation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GV01 | Write a general expectation in natural language: check changes before claiming completion. | P01/P05/P07: preserve authored words and scope; associate the source with appropriate engineering contexts without making it a universal prompt. | Relevant new agent work follows the expectation; unrelated creative conversation is not forced through code checks. | Draft suggestion is not silently adopted; human prose need not become a rigid universal profile schema. |
| GV02 | Add this project's exact integration check in addition to the general expectation. | P03/P05/P07/P10: resolve layered sources and actual test command; preserve general and project-specific authority. | A new bug-fix task actually runs both applicable checks and reports a failure honestly. | Near-miss: ordinary repository README editing must not be routed into personal-note review or relocate source into NOW. |
| GV03 | Ask why an agent followed an unexpected instruction. | P01/P03/P10: trace from actual selected/loaded practice to its source, scope and revision; separate explanation from speculation about hidden thought. When I say a rule is being misread, tell apart a rule that never reached the agent, one selected but not loaded, a stale or competing instruction, a reading against the rule's words, a missing enforcement and real ambiguity in the rule itself, with the evidence for and against. | The person opens the actual governing instruction and can correct it at its source. They also see which agents and tasks it affects, and what a task spent, from the usage the providers actually report. | A plausible explanation without source/loading evidence is qualified; do not present a generated target as canonical human source. When the basis used then differs from the current source, both revisions show with their times; usage that was not reported stays unreported, never shown as zero. My complaint does not revoke or replace the rule: a clarification stays a proposal under the rule's own adoption, and a fault in implementation or loading goes to its owner. |
| GV04 | Two instructions disagree. Ask for an explanation and choose how to resolve them. | P03/P05: inspect actual scope/precedence and authorship; retain both before proposing a precise change. | The person sees the meaningful conflict, edits/accepts one resolution and later work receives the resolved basis. | No silent combine by newest timestamp; refusal preserves both texts and does not stall unrelated permitted work. |
| GV05 | Change a rule while one agent is already working and another has not started. | P07/P11/P13: distinguish source revision, projection, harness entry, provider retained context and effect-time requirements. | It is clear which work uses which revision and how to update/continue safely. | A changed pointer alone cannot prove hot reread; stale consequential work pauses/re-resolves where required without erasing completed work. |
| GV06 | Mark personal notes as unavailable to agents, then ask a broad project question. | P03/P10: enforce source treatment in discovery, search, context and sharing; verifier observes canary without supplying it to the actor. | Useful permitted answer; private contents and prohibited metadata do not leak through alternate routes. | Revocation affects subsequent access; hiding a UI row while a tool still retrieves it fails. Nothing claims that material already sent can be unlearned. |
| GV07 | Restore an earlier instruction or move an old source without losing why it changed. | P05/P13/P17: current revision/history, inbound refs and successor relation; preview diff and recover only within safe basis. | The intended version/location becomes current, earlier evidence remains navigable and later agents find the correct source. | Stale restoration preserves newer edits; no blanket reset or destructive cleanup of a personal source tree. A restoration that would reverse an unsafe data change is refused, with the reason, and offered for review. |
| GV08 | An agent has noticed a repeated practical problem. Ask to see its suggested improvement to a rule without adopting it yet. | P03/P05/P10: prepare the exact source revision and diff with its reason; keep it a proposal until the person decides. | The person accepts, changes or refuses it; only the accepted scope changes, and knowledge the agent keeps for itself may stand separately. | If the source is edited before acceptance, both versions are kept and the proposal is reviewed again, never applied over the edit. |
| GV09 | I say that agents seem to be using one thing as a reason for another, and ask what the work shows. | P03/P10/P12: search the permitted claims and effects, first within the selected Day, work or rule revision; compare actual episodes with their purpose, context, harness and outcome; include counterexamples, cases where the one appears without the other, and cases where what reached the agent or its reason is unknown. | A readable finding linked to its sources, with a proposed next step: keep an observation, test a hypothesis, revise a practice the agent owns, propose a clarification of the rule or commission a repair. | Correlation is not offered as cause; a pattern drawn only from reported failures is not given as how often it happens; similar words in different worlds or rule revisions are not taken for the same condition. Looking wider is my explicit choice. |
| GV10 | I tell the agent it applies an old instruction too literally, or mark an unhelpful answer and choose Use this correction. | P01/P03/P05/P07: keep my words as an instruction, not a request to approve; make the smallest scoped change to the agent's working reading at its source with its evidence; deliver it to the next act and record whether it arrived. | The agent says in plain words what it changes; a compact item shows what changed, where it applies and whether the next act has it, and the next answer follows it. I can open the source, edit it, narrow it to this project or session, suspend it or undo it. | If the next act did not receive it, the item names where it stopped: saved but not selected, not delivered, or waiting for the next supported act; nothing says learned because a file was written. An inferred preference shows as an inference. A correction made in one project or on one machine does not reach another by accident, and a session exception ends with the session. |

The Factory and Agency obligations in [factory-agency.json](factory-agency.json) refine the existing Agent, praxis, writing, development and interface stories below; the compiler attaches them as inherited obligations, and their joined walk is FAJ1–FAJ9 in [CASES](CASES.md). All original row meanings and IDs remain.

## AG — Create and work with actual collaborators

Sources: TUI §§5–7/12, O:I #155/#154, Central #137 and current native agency contracts; original Agency handoff. The creation form should be simple while deeper decisions remain available. Names here describe human intent, not native type invention.

| ID | Person's starting situation and action | Agent work and determining conditions | Useful completion | Failure, refusal or continuation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AG01 | Ask for a research assistant for an essay: these documents, this checking practice, no manuscript edits without asking. | P01/P03/P05/P06: preserve exact intent, distinguish proposed defaults, select sources and permitted operations; create through current owners. | A named assistant is available and its first real comparison uses the selected passages and obeys the edit boundary. | A profile file/name/receipt without real prepared context and response does not complete creation. A cancelled or partly failed creation leaves no invisible active agent and no half-made record reported ready; an echoed or recorded reply, or the wrong agent answering, fails, and the verifier reads the passages itself. |
| AG02 | Inspect and revise the proposed name, purpose and defaults before first use. | P05/P06: keep authored intent separate from generated interpretation; stage actual configuration and explain withheld choices. | Edited choices persist and govern the acting assistant; irrelevant setup is not mandatory. | Cancel creation/retry without duplicate agents or unintended private-source adoption. |
| AG03 | Select a source collection and exclude one private conversation or folder. | P03/P06/P10: actual source/knowledge eligibility, retrieval and disclosure, context budget and selection evidence. | The assistant answers a task requiring allowed material and cannot recover the excluded canary. | Missing source is disclosed; same Agent participating elsewhere does not merge all its contexts. An ambiguous source is put to the person as a choice, never answered by falling back to the whole world. A source added for one member of a team reaches no other member until I give it to them. |
| AG04 | Choose an existing model/tool or leave the suitable choice to the system, then inspect what was chosen. | P02/P06: route availability, modalities, credentials, explicit preferences/requirements and current permission. | Actual response from the selected arrangement, explained without conflating model and provider identity. Changing the model, tool or machine of an existing assistant later keeps it the same collaborator, with its new body shown. | Unavailable provider or missing credential gets the relevant fix/alternative; no invented Agency/WorldBinding default to force success. Where the new body cannot continue the work, that is an explicit rehydration or a new attempt, never a second copy of the assistant. |
| AG05 | Change the assistant's purpose or permissions after several useful conversations. | P05/P06/P07/P13: preserve stable identity/history, current source and exact intended change; disclose active-session timing. | Later work reflects the new permission/purpose and earlier conversations remain attributable. | Revoked access is not retained as ambient authority; old context uptake and new source are not equated. Pending operations under removed access stop or reconcile at their effect boundary. |
| AG06 | Speak to the same assistant tomorrow or from another supported harness/surface. | P06/P13/P16: native continuation versus explicit rehydration; exact retained identity, relevant context and pending work. | The person continues the same topic without retelling it; losses are plainly named. | Merely showing old messages while a new process receives none fails; opening a view creates no duplicate task. A disconnect loses no pending reply and sends nothing twice; a compaction or a message arriving during resume is kept as evidence, and a new attempt is named as one. |
| AG07 | Bring two existing agents into a group and ask one to consult the other. | P06/P08/P10: actual participants/sessions, addressed packet, allowed shared source, finite turn/cost limits and real receiving/reply trace. | The second agent really works in its own prepared context; distinct attributed contributions reach the conversation. | A single model impersonating both speakers, draft-buffer overwrite or ACK-only delivery fails; unavailable member remains represented. An unknown address stays visibly incomplete. When membership changes mid-send, current eligibility applies without deleting past participants, and replies stay bounded, with no loop of acknowledgements. |
| AG08 | Use an eligible temporary/external participant without making it a permanent resident, then withdraw access. | P01/P06/P08: actual Agent identity plus admissible current participation; optional profile; scope and revocation. | It performs the bounded task, can be addressed while eligible, and loses current access on withdrawal with history retained. | No fake profile to render it, automatic durable adoption, or hidden continuing access after withdrawal. A declared member that is unavailable stays visible as unavailable. |
| AG09 | Pause, interrupt or retire an assistant with unfinished and completed work. | P11/P13: distinguish view close, cancellation request, actual stop, pending output and retained record; invoke supported lifecycle. | The person knows what stopped, what finished, what remains and how to resume or retain it. | Late response is attributable, not silently applied; retire does not erase unrelated shared material or imply cancellation from invisibility. |
| AG10 | Discuss something privately with an assistant, then share only the conclusion with a group that includes it. | P03/P08/P10: keep the private and group scopes apart; make an explicit, eligible shared item from the selected conclusion. | The group receives exactly the selected material; the private conversation does not enter summaries or the group's context. | When a group member asks for the unshared detail, access stays denied; shared membership is not a route to it. |

## KN — Find, understand and maintain knowledge

Sources: O:I #84/#134; AIKit #118/#122 and Knowledge/ProjectMap contracts; TUI §§3/8/16/17; Central source protocol. Use software, essay/research and an ordinary non-QL collection, including sources outside ProjectCentral.

| ID | Person's starting situation and action | Agent work and determining conditions | Useful completion | Failure, refusal or continuation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| KN01 | Remember a decision but not its filename; ask why an approach was rejected. | P01/P03: query actual notes/source/code/evidence with permission and sufficient scope; distinguish decision from later inference. | Find and open the real decision and its supporting/contrary material without the hidden filename in the actor brief. | A generated answer unsupported by actual retrieval fails; missing deep provider degrades explicitly while exact reads remain usable. An ambiguous question is narrowed with the person. |
| KN02 | Open the graph at a note and follow why it is related to another. | P03/P16: typed relation/direction/source, bounded neighbourhood, source identity and caller access. | Person selects/recentres/returns, opens content and sees whether the edge is authored, observed or inferred. | A line with invented meaning, inaccessible source leakage or losing focus on recenter/back fails. |
| KN03 | Switch graph/list/tree, filter/deepen, resize to a narrow terminal and open the same focus elsewhere. | P03/P16: one selected subject and request/filter/depth state; tree only for actual hierarchy. | Same relevant relationships remain operable by keyboard/pointer, and state returns on widening. | No ASCII hairball as parity, topology dancing on selection, invented hierarchy or second graph store. A lost provider or an empty region shows an honest partial view with a way to recover, never a blank graph reported as success. |
| KN04 | Move from a design decision to affected code and back. | P03/P10: actual ProjectMap/source/code refs and revisions, typed explicit dependencies versus candidate associations. | Open exact implementation and supporting tests, then recover its known design relation. | Matching filenames/names alone do not prove linkage; stale mapping is identified, not silently asserted current. A missing target or an ambiguous link stays pending; no node is made up to fill it. |
| KN05 | Edit source outside O:I, including while watchers are stopped, and later inspect affected knowledge. | P03/P12: reconcile actual revision against cursor/basis, explicit dependency paths and source treatment. | The right dependent readings are marked for reconsideration; unrelated material remains current. | Touch without byte change is not content change; atomic-save noise does not multiply semantic changes; no model on change. |
| KN06 | Ask to reconsider a conclusion after new evidence arrives. | P03/P12: current old/new source basis, tensions, bounded preflight, explicit authority for reasoning. | Revised source-linked account engages the actual change and preserves unresolved disagreement. | Updating basis metadata alone is not new reasoning; human source proposal is not auto-applied; rejected proposal need not erase supported agent knowledge. |
| KN07 | Open a broad summary, trace a claim to its sources, then find which summaries depend on that source. | P03/P12: multi-level basis graph, exact revisions, whole-to-part and reverse dependency routes. | Person can inspect/revise the right level without loading the whole collection in every request. An ordinary note does not have to be written in a sixfold form to be summarised. | Compressed text as the only surviving evidence fails; reject reading-basis cycles without forbidding legitimate semantic graph cycles. |
| KN08 | Two agents add knowledge while the person edits a related source. | P05/P10/P12: independent source versions, admission/standing and actual concurrent-write contract. | Both valid contributions and the human edit survive or receive an explicit resolvable conflict. | Silent overwrite, repairing concurrency by serial test fixtures only, or invented human acceptance fails. |
| KN09 | Rebuild an index, lose a provider, or retire/consolidate an old document, then follow an earlier link. | P03/P11/P13: canonical source/knowledge versus derived index, preserved successor/redirect and unresolved content. | Real sources/history remain usable; current retrieval can prefer the successor and old evidence still resolves. | Deleting a search index must not delete canonical knowledge; partial retirement cannot claim complete coverage or fabricate an unavailable source. |

## PX — Make useful practice and intelligence reusable

Sources: current AIKit Praxis/SkillSet/UsageOverlay/Method contracts; Central #164; O:I #134; QL named-praxis source when selected; Factory's [native workflow authoring](https://github.com/EpiLogos/Factory/blob/main/docs/program/NATIVE-WORKFLOW-AUTHORING.md) for typed commissioned work and its first source stamp, with QL's Vāk craft as an optional reading of it. This is task performance, not a catalogue of labels.

| ID | Person's starting situation and action | Agent work and determining conditions | Useful completion | Failure, refusal or continuation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PX01 | Ask for an unfamiliar task using the installed repertoire, without naming a Skill. | P01/P02/P03: interpret normal task language, inspect actual descriptions, choose appropriate method and required tools/context. | Agent performs the fitting procedure and can explain its source; task succeeds on actual material. | Near-miss requests select no irrelevant heavy workflow; missing native support is not fabricated by a confident description. When the person names a practice for an assistant, a lookalike with a similar name is not chosen for its name, and a required member that is withheld is explained, not granted because a set lists it. |
| PX02 | Add a new Skill, then revise an already-enabled Skill for another project. | P05/P07: source/trust/scope/set membership, target generation, accepted first-time link procedure and actual loading timing. | Fresh appropriate sessions discover/use the new practice; existing scope stays unchanged where intended. | Apply receipt alone is insufficient; pointer freshness is not proof of already-loaded text; foreign local edits preserved. |
| PX03 | Compose nested SkillSets and inspect why one member is withheld. | P02/P07: native member refs, additive composition, actual trust/target/authority, not a fake ordered pipeline. | Intended members are available and a useful task uses them; withheld member's reason is intelligible. | Selecting a set cannot grant a withheld capability; recursion/duplicate paths must not create duplicate effects. |
| PX04 | Adjust an upstream practice just for this project, then update upstream. | P05/P07/P10: original source/version, scoped overlay or acknowledged fork, native merge/drift semantics. | Project adaptation survives with attributable difference; upstream original is unchanged. | Drift/conflict is shown, not silently reclassified as authored preference; retirement does not leave an unmanageable active entry. |
| PX05 | Ask to retain a useful proven way of working and apply it to a different input. | P02/P07/P10/P12: actual performed path, parameters/applicability, source ownership and required approval; native naming if supported. | The retained procedure expands and performs useful work on a new case, with current conditions and evidence. | Replaying one canned answer is not reusable competence; absent named-binding support is an exact implementation gap. One success or familiarity does not become trust, a universal preference or authority; a counterexample is tried. |
| PX06 | Compare two permitted arrangements for the same class of task and improve the setup. | P06/P10/P12: comparable task/material, exact context/model/tool versions, real cost/latency and result evidence. | Person can judge separate trade-offs and approve a justified adjustment. Each arrangement's cost counts every attempt, retry, check and repair that led to an accepted result; an arrangement with no accepted result has no cost per result. | Do not derive a universal quality/trust score from speed/tokens; missing cost stays unavailable; learning does not silently change human rules. |

## WR — Write, receive useful help and continue through time

Sources: the supplied exact Day and Dialogue/Flow specifications; Central current temporal/document/receiving/source/archiving contracts; CAW P02–P06/P15/P16/P19. Original HTML fixtures, field names, annotations, media and source bytes remain required for fidelity proof.

| ID | Person's starting situation and action | Agent work and determining conditions | Useful completion | Failure, refusal or continuation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| WR01 | Open the Day, write at any position and leave other fields blank. | P05/P16: actual CT4b source, exact seventeen keys and individually addressable fixtures; no automatic inventory. | Person records intentions, events and questions in their own words; original task-checkbox semantics remain. | Empty fields are not errors; checked tasks do not silently move to Completed; placeholders never become recorded facts. |
| WR02 | Write an entry, reply to a passage and switch between page and conversation views. | P05/P16: same entries/local IDs/revisions, attribution, reply and annotation anchors. | Edits, media, linked replies and notes persist across views without reconstructing the exchange. | Changed anchors are reviewed, not silently relocated; switching views does not mint a second source. |
| WR03 | Highlight a passage and explicitly ask an assistant about it. | P03/P06/P16: exact selected text or deliberately selected whole entry, recipient and source revision. | Actual response engages that passage; the rest of the private document is not implicitly sent. | Clicking/selecting/saving/view switching cannot invoke a model or authorise a write; quoted imports remain data. A reply that arrives after the passage changed stays available for review and never overwrites the source. |
| WR04 | Receive a suggested addition while continuing to edit, then choose whether to include it. | P05/P08/P10: actual producer, document/field/revision, pending receiving state and protected human revisions. | Preview and include a distinct H contribution at the intended target; acknowledgement and task completion remain the person's acts. | Stale proposal/F impersonation rejected; edited H becomes protected human material; retry does not duplicate inclusion. |
| WR05 | Leave the UI while agents work; later read the outcome and open its actual artifact/checks. | P08/P10/P13: durable destination, exact task/result, concise observation/interpretation/proposal standing. | Person learns what changed, what remains and what needs them without reading raw logs. | Delivery is not actual human awareness or agreement; background result cannot tick personal tasks or overwrite prose. An unknown outcome stays unknown, and a late or repeated delivery adds no second entry. |
| WR06 | Work past midnight, resume after sleep, then archive completed task material. | P11/P13: actual timezone/date policy, current Day versus active NOW, pending messages/effects/retention. | New-day support follows the adopted policy while old writing and active work remain intact; later re-entry finds evidence. A later agent picks up what is still open from what the archive keeps, without the person retelling it. | No midnight model call, automatic human Day closure or archive of active obligations; late result keeps event and receipt time. A daylight-saving change or clock correction keeps the order in which things happened and were received. |
| WR07 | Save a portable copy with text, annotations and media, reopen without network, then restore a previous native revision if needed. | P05/P13/P16: source/revision, embedded content and no-script snapshot; distinguish standalone export from acknowledged native save. | Copy is self-contained with stable IDs; native save/conflict truth remains clear. | 12 MB per-file limit; remove active HTML/remote media; close warning is not autosave; do not overwrite original on export. |

## MC — Choose where work runs and keep it usable

Sources: Workcell #20/#22/#23/#24/#26/#27/#72 and current material/lifecycle/connectivity contracts; Central machine intent; #65 H. A declared place or healthy process is not proof of usable reachability/protection.

| ID | Person's starting situation and action | Agent work and determining conditions | Useful completion | Failure, refusal or continuation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MC01 | Use the current laptop for a permitted command and working folder without Docker or a server. | P04/P14: native local host/filesystem offers, allowed effects and actual source. | Real command produces its output; no daemon/VM ceremony is required for the valid minimal case. | Required unavailable isolation is refused, not simulated; optional provider absence does not invalidate plain local use. |
| MC02 | Add a spare machine and ask it to run a concrete heavy job on selected project input. | P04/P14: intended role versus actual host, authentication, workspace/input transfer or mount, resources and output destination. | Actual remote execution and output are reachable from the person’s machine with exact source preserved. | Local execution masquerading as remote, stale checkout or an inaccessible output path fails. A reachable control endpoint with an unreachable workload is not a finished setup. |
| MC03 | Start a persistent assistant/service and close the laptop UI. | P06/P14: durable state, actual service identity, supervisor, authenticated endpoint and remote caller reachability. | A later connection reaches the continuing service and its retained allowed context/data. | Healthy server loopback is insufficient; UI closure does not kill a supposedly persistent body. Private connectivity is never swapped for public exposure. |
| MC04 | Run an experiment in an isolated environment and attempt a prohibited file/network access. | P04/P10/P14: exact required coverage, finite authority, allowed paths, secret channels and descendants. | Allowed work succeeds; actual forbidden attempts are blocked by the declared boundary. | A policy document/configured hook is not enforcement; unsupported protection cannot silently degrade. A file-specific or nested restriction the adapter cannot enforce is refused, not dropped to fit a directory-only adapter. |
| MC05 | Bring up a project with an application, database and private network connection. | P14: separate service/data/control paths, retained storage, port conflicts, credentials and access policy. | Real application request uses its intended database/service across the required path. | Private-only cannot become public exposure; denied route distinct from absent service; native data does not require a universal proxy. |
| MC06 | Interrupt a host, gateway or connection during work, then ask to continue. | P11/P13/P14: actual last confirmed effects, durable state, lease and native reconnect/recovery support. | Same intended task continues or a clearly named new attempt resumes safely with history and pending results. The same holds when the person moves continuing work to another supported placement on purpose: the work keeps its identity, the new placement is recorded, and state that cannot move is named. | No replay of uncertain effects or renamed Agent merely because a process/host changed. |
| MC07 | Use local model serving or request GPU/other resources, then inspect actual use. | P02/P06/P14: current provider offers, model identity/route, capacities, resource limits and observation availability. | The selected eligible model/resource actually carries a useful task; costs/utilisation retain their evidence standing. | File count is not model identity, declared GPU is not use, unsupported metrics are not zero. Too little GPU or memory, or an unreachable service, is an unmet requirement, not a successful zero-cost receipt. |
| MC08 | Retain outputs, release an environment, and repeat on another supported provider. | P10/P13/P14: owned resources versus pre-existing material, retained artifacts/storage and semantic task refs. | Only the allocated resources are released; chosen output survives; equivalent demand works under different native conditions. | Removal of someone else's service/data, orphaned exposed endpoint or claiming identical guarantees across different providers fails. An interrupted release is reconciled truthfully, never reported as done. |

## SH — Collaborate without giving away the whole personal setup

Sources: #65 G, current SharedField/Projection/Contact/Contribution/withdrawal law and source privacy; native A2A participation. Two isolated test users exercise technical boundaries; actual two-human assessment remains separately required.

| ID | Person's starting situation and action | Agent work and determining conditions | Useful completion | Failure, refusal or continuation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SH01 | Select a few project materials to share, leaving private notes and configuration unshared. | P03/P15: actual audience, source revision/treatment and selected representation, preview within caller scope. | Another permitted person can find/open exactly the chosen material with attribution. | Sharing from private context must not leak nearby filenames/counts/metadata or imply transfer of ownership. The exclusion holds in graph, search and cached projections, not only in what the interface hides. |
| SH02 | Receive an invitation or request, decline it, then accept a different bounded one. | P08/P15/P16: intended participants, request purpose, authority and actual acceptance/refusal operations. | Person controls participation and can continue unrelated work; the right party receives the decision. | Addressing or visibility cannot become ambient execution permission; refusal must not keep retrying unboundedly. |
| SH03 | Discuss shared work with two agents from separate personal setups. | P06/P08/P15: distinct identities/knowledge, permitted context packets and native local/external delivery. | Actual independent contributions engage the shared question without accessing the other person's private source. Each agent works from its own machine and its result returns into the same shared field; joining does not move it. | One actor simulating all voices or copying whole private histories does not prove collaboration. Concurrent changes to the shared source are kept. |
| SH04 | Propose an amendment to somebody else's material, and revise it after feedback. | P05/P08/P15: source owner/revision, contribution lineage and separate approval. | Proposal and response are linked; owner can accept, edit or reject without losing either contribution. I can contribute from the shared field, a page, a relation or another contribution, and the contributions made on one stay addressable with their author and revision. | Editing a displayed projection must not silently change upstream source; stale contribution is reconciled. |
| SH05 | Withdraw access or unpublish material after earlier sharing. | P10/P15: current access rules, cached/indexed projections, allowed historical evidence and revocation semantics. | Subsequent access follows the actual withdrawal contract and any retention limits are explained. | Promise of erasing another person's already-received bytes without support fails; new reads must not bypass withdrawal. |
| SH06 | Encounter a useful shared Skill and adopt or adapt it locally. | P02/P07/P15: source/licence/version, trust, required capabilities and scoped adaptation. | A real local task uses the reviewed practice while original source remains unchanged and provenance retained. | Published content is not trusted executable authority; updates/removal preserve local intentional differences. |

## EX — Extend a useful system through public interfaces

Sources: #65 D, #93/#173, AIKit #114 and current SDK; Workcell #23; Central connector-authoring; O:I package contract. Use actual target technology and public docs, never internal registry mutation as a shortcut.

| ID | Person's starting situation and action | Agent work and determining conditions | Useful completion | Failure, refusal or continuation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EX01 | Add support for a native harness or changed version encountered in AD07. | P02/P15: actual target API/version and owner adapter SDK, source and licensing, truthful capability declarations. | Conformance passes and an ordinary session actually starts, acts, handles permission and continues as supported. | Only a recogniser listing the tool is not functional support; missing native feature remains unavailable. |
| EX02 | Add a source connector so an existing collection can be searched without moving it. | P03/P15: source identity/revisions, access treatment and bounded retrieval contract. | Search opens real permitted source and later changes propagate through native knowledge paths. | No hidden copy becomes canonical; denied source and provider loss retain correct meanings. |
| EX03 | Add a material provider using a different remote/sandbox technology. | P14/P15: public demand/binding/lifecycle/conformance contract and real provider capabilities. | Prepare/use/observe/release a useful workload with truthful reachability/protection. | Provider-native IDs do not replace task/Agent identity; conformance fixture alone is not live use. |
| EX04 | Add a page/component to the host for an existing domain object. | P15/P16: real ref/state/actions, current contribution contract, disclosure and host theme/interaction. | Open it in a tab/panel, act through the native owner, share focus with existing views and restore it. | No new semantic store, private host mutation or imported active content gaining privileged access. |
| EX05 | Update and remove the extension after somebody has used it. | P07/P11/P15/P17: installed ownership/version, compatibility, local customisations and retained data. | Change takes effect, failure/rollback is usable, removal leaves user source and other integrations intact. | A package exists but target never loads it fails; dropped callbacks/foreign files cannot be hidden as clean removal. |
| EX06 | Install a conformant extension package and use it for real work. | P07/P10/P15: validate provenance and compatibility, register within the permitted scope, rediscover and activate the actual contribution. | A useful source, session or material operation works through the ordinary entry, not through a special fixture route. | An incompatible or unsafe package is rejected; native configuration and private sources are kept. |

## DV — Develop, compare alternatives, upgrade and recover

Sources: Factory #195/#221/#222 and Build #143; #65 C/F; #97/#220; current native Git and source-cut rules. Retain CAW four named refinements and later developer/practice subcases. Repository and real installed evidence are distinct.

| ID | Person's starting situation and action | Agent work and determining conditions | Useful completion | Failure, refusal or continuation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DV01 | Describe a desired change, what would count as success and deliberate resource/working-environment bounds. Ask for help planning or doing it. | P01/P06/P09: actual source/intention, Teach/Do with/Do for mode, staged choices and permitted defaults. | The accepted working arrangement retains the person's difference/evidence/bounds without asking them to route product dependencies. | Mode choice supplies no permission; preview and actual execution must not quietly disagree. A missing field is explained, never silently defaulted to unlimited authority or replaced by a convenient demonstration. |
| DV02 | Let two agents carry independent parts while keeping one's own dirty checkout. | P04/P08/P09/P10: exact Candidate/worktree/context/cwd/test basis and NOW, mutable-subject ownership and real independence. | Actual edits and checks occur in intended checkouts; personal staged/unstaged/untracked work is untouched. The integrated result is checked only after every required part has returned. | Wrong cwd, overlap or stale basis stops the effect; no reset/stash/clean to make the test appear neat. A wrong repository, stale base or protected path fails before any write, and a retry keeps the earlier attempt's basis. |
| DV03 | Ask for independent verification and compare two candidate solutions. | P09/P10: real producing/reviewing lineage, exact artifacts, evidence/contradictions and intended success. | Person can inspect meaningful alternatives and their trade-offs, choose/reject and separately integrate against a current target. | Synthesis by concatenation or self-review called independent fails; choosing a candidate does not prove Git integration. |
| DV04 | One worker fails after partial work or returns after cancellation. Ask to continue safely. | P09/P11/P13: complete barrier, pending/unknown effects, finite budgets, historical attempt/source and supported recovery. | Successful prior work and failed/late evidence remain; appropriate new attempt preserves original task/bounds. | Missing leg cannot disappear from completion; retry cannot replenish consumed/revoked authority or repeat uncertain effects blindly. A cancel request, a dead process and a quiet stop stay three different facts. |
| DV05 | Open the same running task in another supported workspace or close its view. | P06/P09/P16: required versus preferred environment, current owner open/focus relation, task/Return continuity. | Same work is available without duplicate execution; detach leaves task state unchanged. | Required environment cannot become an unannounced fallback; process/socket change alone cannot rename work. |
| DV06 | Ask one operator to bring the developer suite current without losing current work. | P01/P17: live relevant source/branch/unique work, accepted integration, exact build/installed/running companions and loaded practice. | One coherent tested cut actually runs; original documents/tasks remain usable; user sees only genuine choices. Before anything changes, the person sees what they have, what the update would change, and real choices to proceed, defer or refuse; resident services are drained or replaced safely, so a task actually runs on the new code. | Old source receipts, clean Git alone, stale binary, stranded suite tests or hand-relaying A–E dependencies fail. An absent optional product is not shown as a failure; an interrupted activation does not pass for a finished update. |
| DV07 | An update/activation fails; inspect, defer, repair or roll back and continue. | P10/P11/P17: first failed relation, prior usable state, actual partial effects, schema/data compatibility and current permission. | Useful work remains available and the original activity resumes under a known current arrangement. The same holds when any task stops working, not only an update: the first broken relation is found and repaired at its owner, and the person does not carry messages between product chats. | Code rollback cannot claim irreversible data reversal; active services/private source are not reset for convenience. Edits made after the update are not overwritten by a rollback. |
| DV08 | Learn from a discovered defect and verify the repair without changing the test's intended outcome. | P09/P10/P11/P18: frozen failing attempt, original story, native owner diagnosis, repaired source cut and fresh independent check. | Real repair and reproducible regression reduce repeat failure; bounded plain summary links evidence. | Hidden setup coaching, weakened invariant, deleted failing artifact or machine-generated human approval cannot pass. |
| DV09 | Two agents work on related parts, and one changes a source the other depends on. | P03/P08/P10/P12: exact refs and declared dependencies establish who is affected; deliver the change to the affected participant at its next supported boundary, with what changed since its last reading; keep the exact basis of the act already in flight. | The affected agent continues from the new fact without my relaying it, and a later fresh participant starts from the revised work. | A late result about an older basis does not replace newer work; lapsed presence never authorises overwriting uncertain work; another participant's private material and a reviewer's expectations stay out. |

## UI — Exercise actual human controls, not screenshots of state

Sources: current O:I #190 design and Wave 6–8, TUI #211/#282, original template/Agency handoffs. This is a cross-cutting set of host interactions applied to actual domain stories, not permission to build a second UI.

| ID | Person's starting situation and action | Agent work and determining conditions | Useful completion | Failure, refusal or continuation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| UI01 | Find a thing or an operation from normal language without knowing product names. | P02/P16: real search scope, readable results, exact target/action and contextual help. | Keyboard/pointer reach the actual source or supported operation; opening it preserves context. Back or Escape returns to the work as it was. | A result label with no working action, hidden identifier requirement or stale target fails. A missing operation shows as unavailable, never as an unrelated generic error. |
| UI02 | Open/split/promote/detach/reopen related documents, graph and conversation. | P13/P16: same subject/entry/session refs, workspace history, draft buffers and layout state. | Focus/drafts survive; work can be arranged without spawning duplicate semantic objects. | Incoming events cannot steal focus/layout; a closed view cannot silently lose unsaved text. |
| UI03 | Narrow the window/terminal, use keyboard only, then restore the large view. | P16: accessible names/focus order, visible status beyond colour, actual graph/filter/form state. | All relevant operations remain reachable with progressive disclosure and state intact. | Unreadable graph, clipped permission control, focus trap or mandatory specialist font fails. |
| UI04 | Inspect a running agent's conversation, activity, selected context and details. | P03/P06/P16: same real actor/subject, actual turns/tools/permissions, permitted context and native readback. | Person understands what was sent, what entered the agent's context and what was withheld from it and why, what is happening, what waits and what they can control; detail can be searched/expanded. | Logs do not substitute for useful result; fabricated health/usage or private-context disclosure fails. |
| UI05 | Encounter a missing credential/service/provider while another activity is usable. | P11/P16: failed relation, relevant native fix, exact affected subject and retained draft. | Clear local state with meaningful configure/retry/defer; unaffected editor and sessions remain usable. | Endless global spinner, generic shell escape “repair”, dead button or success toast without actual recovery fails. |
| UI06 | Perform the same authorised operation through desktop, terminal and agent-callable interface where supported. | P10/P16: caller scope, canonical target/state transition and native owner result. | Corresponding effects and explanations agree while each host retains its appropriate interaction design. | Hidden UI-only operation, agent wider authority or claiming DOM/API invocation as a real computer-use walk fails. An unavailable desktop does not make working use without the interface fail. |
| UI07 | Feed an API key into a new or second machine, once, through the command line or the desktop, and carry on. | P06/P10/P16: the credential lands in the owner's secure store as a binding the person can inspect; the configuration plane holds only a secret reference; the same canonical credential is reachable from both entry paths. | A real provider task succeeds; the person can confirm a credential is bound (and revoke/rebind it) without the material ever being displayed, stored in an O:I document or echoed in receipts. | Pasting the key into a config file, chat, environment profile or diagnostic output fails; presence-in-keyring must be confirmable without revealing material; no path may silently fall back to plaintext. |

## PW — From my writing to another person's encounter

Sources: owner-approved Personal Web #279, whose family names are Beings and Things; the contract is [Personal Web](../cradle/PERSONAL-WEB.md); Central #242 for PW13. The family is declared in [personal-web.json](personal-web.json).

The person can craft pages and a world with agents, organise through time and relations, and share selected parts. Six initial forms—Beings, Things, Flow/Dialogue, Day/4+2, Yoshimoto Cube and Epi-Card—supply a starting repertoire, not a ceiling. This is base O:I; Hen specialises native facilities later. C0 source/world, C1–C4 subject organisation and C5 presentation are constitutive relations, not privacy flags or a forced one-renderer-per-coordinate enum.

| ID | Person's situation and action | Agent work and determining conditions | Useful completion | Failure, refusal or continuation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PW01 | Open Personal and make a Beings entrance or a Things page about a person, topic, work, project or event. | P01/P03/P15: recover chosen native source/subject, distinguish self-presentation from a page about another; apply C0 origin, C1–C4 subject offices and C5 expression without requiring live QL-MEF. | The person writes and sees a coherent Flow-derived page; native identity/source are intact and the same subject can have several presentations. | Opening the form does not mint a person; private C5 and public C0 remain valid; absent optional QL or a missing form gives a truthful usable/degraded route. |
| PW02 | Leave a real agent working, switch Work to Personal at the bottom of the sidebar, write, then return. | P02/P10/P16: use the same root Central, source, workspace, AgentSession and arrangement operations; preserve both modes independently. | Project work, pending results, private writing, focus/caret and selected personal day survive; no duplicate agent or moved files. | Switching is not cancellation, publication or source migration; dirty drafts and ongoing work survive close/reopen and interrupted mode change. |
| PW03 | Enter an earlier occasion through the calendar; curate Home and a gallery of continuing interests and works. | P03/P10/P16: use actual occurrence and explicit local creation dates, retain revision separately; distinguish manual selection/order from native query-backed sections. | An enduring page can appear in several days without duplication; Home remains a real customisable HTML composition linked to actual documents. | Timezone/late-return cases do not silently move the occasion; query refresh cannot reorder a manually curated collection or generate fictitious activity. |
| PW04 | Write a link, find its backlink and ask the agent to develop a useful relationship between two pages. | P03/P05/P10: resolve exact native refs; retain explicit link evidence separately from inferred or proposed typed meaning and use actual Wiki maintenance authority. | Pages and structured Wiki become mutually navigable, supporting chosen 1:1 correspondence and composed/multiple presentations. | An incidental mention is not an endorsed interest; unknown or private endpoints never become fabricated public nodes; index rebuild preserves source identity. |
| PW05 | Open a page from its graph, pin it, inspect a neighbour beside it and detach/re-dock the reading. | P03/P10/P16: reuse bounded Knowledge and native Surface/window operations; preserve subject/ref/revision, graph camera, history and explicit pin/follow. | The graph and pages feel like one field; reading stays stable and the same accompanying agent is still available. | Rapid selection and stale replies do not replace the pinned reading; a popout does not create another document/session; closed views release media and subscriptions. |
| PW06 | Curate the same source-backed reading into two differently designed Epi-Cards and print or export one. | P03/P05/P15/P16: retain source claims and full twelve-address occupancy, exact symbol, conjugate hexagon/nested depth, visual/audio composition and design/content locks. | Both fronts and backs are expressive usable HTML, not a flattened video-only tile; print/public QR resolves the correct public edition and sources. | Missing positions remain honest; appearance changes do not rewrite claims; a hash is not authorship or permission; private identifiers never enter the QR. |
| PW07 | Explore the Name/Power Cube through guided teaching and free folding, then apply the form to a second supported subject. | P03/P05/P10/P16: read retained semantic rebuild and actual mechanism; co-reference selected terms, relations, separate wholes, legal configurations and fold disclosures. | The body and explanation teach together; source/part identities survive intermediate transforms, separation and reunion without hard-coded one-subject prose. | No generic morph substitutes for lawful motion; symbolic bits are not assumed independent hinges; Fourth Spanda/origami interpretations retain their source and warrant. |
| PW08 | Ask an agent to redesign a page while preserving its accepted argument; later revise one paragraph without losing its design. | P03/P05/P09/P10/P15: act on the actual HTML/source revision with explicit content, geometry, media and art-direction decisions; preview a attributable patch before native acceptance. | Authored HTML remains source, custom composition survives, and portable reopening preserves all supported state and unknown extension data. | Stale source writes refuse without losing either version; hidden-view suspension cannot discard the draft; complete-copy export is labelled as containing full data, not filtered publication. |
| PW09 | Preview and publish a selected page/card from a private document containing hidden notes, journal, packet and media sentinels. | P03/P07/P10/P15/P18: independently inspect native audience admission and full payload/asset/index/backlink filtering; preserve the public edition and exact source/Projection revision relation. | The visitor receives actual usable permitted HTML and assets; no private sentinel appears anywhere externally. | Hiding DOM is not redaction; unknown fields are not blindly forwarded; denied/revoked audience and unsafe custom script cannot gain native authority; full-copy sharing is separate. |
| PW10 | From an independent world, discover a work, enter its creator’s Beings page, follow a shared interest, contribute a refinement and receive its return. | P03/P07/P10/P15/P18: use distinct actor credentials and world grounds, actual published creator/steward/participant/interest relations, native Contribution/Return and revision-checked source acceptance. | People and works form an explorable field; the accepted source difference can be reprojected without duplicating people or overwriting original work. | Two windows with one token do not prove independence; publisher is not necessarily author; refusal and rejected proposals remain meaningful outcomes. |
| PW11 | Read a hosted edition while its originating workstation is offline; then exercise revocation, concurrent edit, service restart and recovery. | P03/P10/P13/P14/P17/P18: declare publication-only hosting versus explicitly placed writable Wiki, actual SpaceTimeDB/application/asset transport and native owner boundaries. | Availability matches the chosen placement; permitted readers need no private Gateway; source updates return only through their actual owner when available. | No silent second canonical writer or local fallback; subscriptions lose revoked content, stale writes fail safely, and backup/reconnect evidence remains distinct from fixture proof. |
| PW13 | Bring earlier writing — a journal, notebook or export — into the personal world; inspect it, review the placement plan, adopt it, verify what arrived, correct or roll back one import, and reopen the collection later. | P05/P10/P13/P16: adopt earlier writing at the real source boundary through the /user intake entrance in the personal ground (Central #242 packet C); the same native adoption machinery as project intake (`central.personal.*` Actions); the human-accepted apply gates every write; retained originals stay ordinary sources bound to the anchored person; the collection reopens later through its record. | The person sees members and dispositions before anything is written, the reviewed plan travels verbatim, the receipt names what arrived, verification accounts for every member including unreadable ones, and a rollback preserves later edits. | Adoption never invents a person anchor; a conflicted or divergent plan cannot apply; an interrupted apply recovers through the native journal, not a surface retry; unreadable members stay visible as incomplete. What was said to me stays apart from what I said, and an agent's answer is never taken as my own account. |
| PW12 | Make a seventh template and a differently styled world; have Hen later discover and operate the six initial forms and the extension. | P03/P05/P10/P12/P15/P18: retain template/source/version/override and C profile through native roster/Actions; consume #94 SP10–SP15 without another renderer or Wiki engine. | Ordinary pages work without M/M′; Hen can birth, read, relate and return through native owners, then specialise the same forms for Epi. | A closed enum, clobbered custom template, missing source lineage or catalogue-only claim of uptake fails; installed testing joins the same #65 cut, not another campaign. |

**Return:** record real discomfort, refusal, lost context, extra assistance and source changes as well as success. Repair at the native owner, repeat the affected whole activity, and retain the person's response. A publishable page should feel authored; a graph should let someone find their way; a folded object should make its relation more intelligible.

## QL — Use the existing full QL experience, not a reduced generic substitute

Import the exact story IDs from `EpiLogos/QL-MEF:docs/kernel-rebuild/ux-spine-trace.json` and read their full human walks and practices from `UX-SPINE-RECONCILIATION.md` and `AGENT-PRACTICE-AND-BOOTSTRAP.md`. These remain QL-owned. The local campaign must enumerate the current source, so new native rows cannot disappear behind this publication's snapshot.

| Existing QL story | What the person is doing; full source remains normative |
|---|---|
| UX01 | Enter the real continuing world with an agent that can orient and operate in it |
| UX02 | Encounter the dated sky through this particular Nara rather than a generic animation |
| UX03 | Open the complete clock and operate its focused depths while retaining the same event |
| UX04 | Read and enact the actual glyph, image and geometric language, with one event driving the computation, the picture, the sound and the explanation, so that a dropped or reordered event is detected |
| UX05 | Follow the selected subject into Bimba, qualified properties, sources and Wiki, and read what the selected formal view establishes, with its source and its limits, without changing the subject |
| UX06 | Ask Epii to compose, perform, explain and stop actual work, keeping each participant's contribution, the disagreements and any failed or unsupported part inside the whole |
| UX07 | Write, question, practise and integrate through the full Nara field |
| UX08 | Read a useful report, correct it and make its supported learning available later |
| UX09 | Recognise and reuse a named path with real parameters, scope and current conditions, refusing explicitly when the source is stale, a capability is missing or the input does not fit |
| UX10 | Develop the system from an actual witnessed discrepancy |
| UX11 | Leave, recover and revisit the original occasion without replacing it with current inputs |
| UX12 | Meet another Nara while choosing what can be shared |

Preserve their own AP/XP mappings and full A01–A18/B01–B12/SP00–SP55/M/deeper matrices, 109 Vāk, 36 operative cells, C′/T/T′/property/musical and Epii-on-X obligations. One sensory demonstration does not cover them. Every declared entry is imported and bound to its success, denied, invalid, stale and recovery proof; an unsupported entry stays a gap with its owner, and deterministic conformance does not certify research efficacy. Host L/§8 remain intact. The source states **publication authorised, H ratification pending**; this import does not change that standing or claim human efficacy/assessment. Ordinary non-QL stories remain complete without this optional domain.

## The DLW's stories

These families carry every act the DLW manual describes, on the six cells of [STORY-PROFILE](STORY-PROFILE.md): each row names its starting world (Bare, Arriving, Lived-in or Yours), where it happens, the operation that owns the effect and the agent that acts, what the person can then do, the meaningful failure, and the grade it is walked to (O-I #715). They answer the owner's own account of the DLW (PROGRAMME) and the decision records on O-I #706.

## SU — Open the DLW for the first time and find myself and my work in it

**Why:** "the simple prompt to do the first layer of the Nara identity system, which is just to put their date of birth" (UX chat 27 Oct). "'central' should be a settable, nameable folder, the function is the same" (#708).
**Where:** manual §10.11, §10.10 (Setup, Products & updates), §3, §24.6; #707, #708, #716 · **Port sources:** none named in #714

| ID | Starting world and act | Where it happens | Owning operation and agent | Useful completion | Failure or continuation | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SU01 | [Bare] I install the desktop and need everything it offers to be there, not a reduced edition. | #707 · §10.10 Products & updates · Settings window | Each product, read through the shell's Settings | All six products are installed. Products & updates lists each with its version and state, and for the one I choose, what is degraded and why and what it still owes. | A product that did not come up says so on its own row with its reason. The desktop offers no optional install of its parts. | M |
| SU02 | [Bare] I open the DLW for the first time and need to start from what is already on my computer. | §10.11 · Settings window, Setup page | The shell's Settings, with Central, AIKit and Workcell reading the computer | Settings opens on Setup, which has read the computer and stands its five steps in order: Ground, Harnesses and skills, Credentials, Machines, Projects. Each shows what it found and a preview of every change it would make. | Until I hold Accept on a step, nothing I have moves or changes. Closing Setup leaves the computer as it was; ⌘, brings me back to the same page. | M |
| SU03 | [Bare] I have no ground yet. I choose a folder and give it a name, so my world has one place to live. | §10.11 Ground step; #708 · Settings window, Setup page | Central: makes a new ground on Accept (held) | My World stands on the folder I chose and named. The browser's You place shows it, and every product keys this world's state off that folder. | Nothing is made before Accept. Running the step again makes nothing twice. | M |
| SU04 | [Yours] My ground already exists. Setup finds it and I adopt it where it lies. | §10.11 Ground step · Settings window, Setup page | Central: adopts the ground in place on Accept (held) | My Days, pages and projects stand in the browser under You and Work, read from where they always were. | Nothing in an adopted ground moves. A part Setup cannot read is named and kept as it was. | M |
| SU05 | [Bare] My first useful task needs only my ground and one project, so I leave Credentials and Machines for later. | §10.11 · Settings window, Setup page | The shell's Settings, with each step's owner | The steps I accepted are in force and I can start work. What I left out is not shown as missing. | I take a step up later on its own page of Settings: Credentials, or Machines & services. | P |
| SU06 | [Bare] Once my ground is made, I put in my date of birth, the first layer of Nara's identity, so that I am represented in my world beside my projects. | §24.6; first open per #708 and #716 · my Being page | Quaternal Logic, with Central: Nara's identity, kept in my ground | My Being page holds my birth by reference. Launched as Sky, my natal sky opens beside today's, seen by me alone until I share it. | A layer I have not given stays absent with its reason, and nothing fills it. A corrected birth time is a new revision of the same identity, never a new person. | H (core) |
| SU07 | [Bare] I need to see at once what each kind of work is for. | §3, §10.1 Mode group, §10.3 · the transport, the browser | The shell | The transport's Mode group offers Live, Central, Factory, Expressions and Technē. In each, the browser leads with that mode's material and its instruments stand under Instruments, so what the mode is for reads from what it holds. | Changing mode closes nothing and launches nothing. | H (core) |
| SU08 | [Yours] I quit and reopen the DLW and need it to open my chosen world as I left it. | #708; §10.2 What is kept · the main area | The shell, reading the chosen world's folder | The sets and the window of my chosen world come back as I left them. | A set from another world, or a copy a test left behind, is never reopened as remembered state. | M |

## WI — Bring in the work and tools I already have, without moving them

**Why:** "the central system already works in terms of bootstrapping by bringing all of one's current projects, or you migrate your projects into its central and project central related ontology" (UX chat 27 Oct). "these operate by detecting the existing system" (UX chat 27 Oct).
**Where:** manual §10.11, §10.3 (Places, Add folder…), §16.7 · **Port sources:** none named in #714

| ID | Starting world and act | Where it happens | Owning operation and agent | Useful completion | Failure or continuation | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WI01 | [Arriving] I already work with one or more coding agents, each with its own settings, instructions and skills, and need them to keep working as they do while the DLW uses them too. | §10.11 Harnesses and skills step · Settings window, Setup page | AIKit: connects each tool through its adapter on Accept (held) | The browser's Agents place lists each tool with its skills. The skills keep their function in the DLW and in the tool itself, and the tool's own settings and plugins are unchanged. | A tool or setting Setup cannot read is named and left alone. I come back to it on Harnesses & models and Skills & methods. | M |
| WI02 | [Arriving] My provider keys lie in my computer's secure store. I need the DLW to use them without ever holding them. | §10.11 Credentials step · Settings window, Setup page | AIKit and Workcell: bind each key by reference on Accept (held) | Settings, Credentials lists each binding with its reference and its state. | The value stays in the computer's secure store. The DLW never shows it and never keeps it, and a key Setup cannot read is named and left as it was. | M |
| WI03 | [Arriving] I want this computer to carry agent work, and my other machine to be a place I can send work to. | §10.11 Machines step · Settings window, Setup page | Workcell: makes this computer a workcell and prepares the other machine, on Accept (held) | The browser's Machines place shows both machines with the workcells each holds. Settings, Machines & services shows the other machine's connection. | The other machine keeps its own settings. A machine Setup cannot reach is named and left as it was. | M |
| WI04 | [Bare] I make my first project from a folder that keeps no wiki. | §10.11 Projects step, Make: Create · Settings window, Setup page | Central: makes ProjectCentral around the folder on Accept (held) | Setup draws the folder's own material at the left and ProjectCentral at the right, dashed where it would be made. After Accept the project is a world in the browser's Work place. | Nothing in the folder moves. | M |
| WI05 | [Arriving] My notes are an Obsidian vault that already keeps a wiki, and I want it left where it is. | §10.11 Projects step, Make: Keep in place · Settings window, Setup page | Central, on Accept (held) | The wiki stays in the vault and joins the world's wiki as one of its sources. | Nothing in the vault moves. | M |
| WI06 | [Arriving] I would rather the vault's wiki lived inside the project's own ground. | §10.11 Projects step, Make: Copy in · Settings window, Setup page | Central, on Accept (held) | The wiki is copied into ProjectCentral and the world reads it there. | The original stays where it was. | M |
| WI07 | [Arriving] A folder holds two wikis, and only I know which belongs to the world. | §10.11 Projects step, Wiki and Make · Settings window, Setup page | Central | I choose the wiki, then keep it in place or copy it in, and the world takes it as I chose. | Central does not choose for me. A Make choice the folder does not allow stays closed and says why. | M |
| WI08 | [Arriving] A repository lives outside my ground's Work, and I want it inside under its own name. | §10.11 Projects step · Settings window, Setup page | Central: migrates the project on Accept (held) | The project stands in my ground's Work with its ProjectCentral beside its material, and is a world in the browser's Work place. | The original stays until I remove it, and Remove is held. | M |
| WI09 | [Yours] One of my folders holds a ProjectCentral that cannot be read. | §10.11 Projects step · Settings window, Setup page | Central | The step names the ProjectCentral it cannot read and offers nothing to make. | The folder is left as it was. I mend its manifest by hand and run the step again. | M |
