| CS02 | [Lived-in] I send a paragraph to a colleague. | §18.4 What a tag sends: Person · a selected passage, @, Enter | Central | A message reaches them carrying the passage and where it came from. | A person in another world gets a request, which waits until they accept it. | P |
| CS03 | [Lived-in] I start an agent on a passage. | §18.4 What a tag sends: Agent · a selected passage, @, Enter | Actuation (the agent), with Central | The agent reads the passage and the packet and starts working on it; pressing Capture as I tag also adds the passage to the packet. | The agent writes only as Scope and Arm allow. | P |
| CS04 | [Lived-in] I file a quote on the place it is about. | §18.4 What a tag sends: Place · a selected passage, @, Enter | Central | The passage is filed on that spot of my world, with a link back to the page. | — | P |
| CS05 | [Lived-in] I add a passage to another document. | §18.4 What a tag sends: Document · a selected passage, @, Enter | Central | The passage joins with a link back: a Flow takes it as a new entry, another document as a quote at the end. | A tag never sends to a passage or a Vāk entry; it opens them. | P |
| CS06 | [Lived-in] I need to see what a tag will send before it goes. | §18.4 What a tag sends · the search line | Central | A line shows what is sent, to whom and its size. Shift and Enter opens the result without sending; Esc closes the line. | A tag always shows what it sends, and its size, before it goes. | P |
| CS07 | [Lived-in] I search from wherever I am, not only in a document. | §18.4 The line anywhere · ⌘F in a pane, @ in a document, ⌘K over anything | Central | The same line with the same language, settings and results opens in each place; where it opens decides where a result goes. | Esc closes it and I am where I was. | P |
| CS08 | [Lived-in] I type a few letters to jump to a place I open often. | §18.4 The line anywhere · the search line | Central | Plain words jump to the best match by how often and how lately I opened it; a chip stands for each part of what I typed that the line read. | Any mark turns the jump into a search. With Jump off, plain words search. | P |
| CS09 | [Lived-in] I want my search history kept to me, or not kept at all. | §18.4 The line anywhere, History · the search line | Central | The history lives in my own ground, showing how often and how lately I opened each item; ✕ forgets an item. | No one else's search sees it, and nothing in it is sent. With History off, the line never ranks by use. | P |
| CS10 | [Lived-in] I widen a search beyond my own world, or narrow what it returns. | §18.4 Parameters: Reach, Kinds, Order, Results shown, On Enter · Search | Central | Reach looks in my world, my projects and agents, the shared field or joined fields; Kinds, Order and Results shown set what comes back; On Enter sets Send or Open. | To be found is not to be reachable; each step belongs to its owner. | P |
| CS11 | [Lived-in] I search from any mode without opening a document. | §18.4 Widget · the Search tile on the rail | Central | Typing @ in the tile searches; clicking an alias runs it. | — | P |
| CS12 | [Lived-in] I put a search I use every day on a key. | §18.4 Transport, Keys and maps · the transport | Central | The alias or phrase runs from its key. | — | P |

## CG — Grow into Search's own language

**Why:** "intuition is just a function of familiarity and curiosity coming together" (UX chat 27 Oct).
**Where:** manual §18.4 (The language, The language of the map, Your own aliases, Five steps to fluency) · **Port sources:** VS Code's workbench (the command line over anything)

| ID | Starting world and act | Where it happens | Owning operation and agent | Useful completion | Failure or continuation | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CG01 | [Lived-in] I aim a search at one horizon and one kind of thing. | §18.4 The language: @0 to @5, kinds · the search line | Central | The search looks only in that horizon (from the knowledge I hold to tools and practices) and only at that kind, such as `agent:` or `source:`. | A horizon is where a search looks, not a tier of a project's Docs. | P |
| CG02 | [Lived-in] I include and leave out terms to shape a search. | §18.4 The language: `+`, `-` · the search line | Central | The results include what I marked with + and leave out what I marked with −. | — | P |
| CG03 | [Lived-in] I relate two terms, or hold several as one expression. | §18.4 The language: `x`, `/`, `=`, `( )` · the search line | Central | One term read by, as or through another; two held together in context; one expressed as another; several framed as one. | — | P |
| CG04 | [Lived-in] I ask what could serve a task, not for one answer. | §18.4 The language: `@#` · the search line | Central | The line shows possibilities; I include the tools I want with +. | — | P |
| CG05 | [Lived-in] I turn a search I repeat into a short name of my own. | §18.4 Your own aliases · the search line | Central | `@name = search` keeps it; `@name` runs it; a `?` leaves a gap the next word fills; an alias can name a group of targets to tag at once. | An alias is mine; another person's alias does not run in my Search. | P |
| CG06 | [Lived-in] I read what a coordinate of the map says, among its neighbours. | §18.4 The language of the map · Search, Find page | Quaternal Logic (the Vāk registry), with Central | `vak:` and the coordinate open the entry with its parent above and the entries one relation away round it; raising Vāk depth, or dragging the handle out, brings in the second ring. | A Vāk entry is found by its coordinate, never by a word in its name, and its result reaches two relations round at most. | P |

## CD — Keep each world's own account of itself

**Why:** "So the central documentation approach is completely built on quaternal logic." (UX chat 27 Oct)
**Where:** manual §10.4 (the docs house), §18.5 · **Port sources:** Obsidian 1.7.7 (graph and links); VS Code's workbench (the code-editor side)

| ID | Starting world and act | Where it happens | Owning operation and agent | Useful completion | Failure or continuation | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CD01 | [Yours] I need a project's own account of itself before anyone guesses what it is for. | §18.5 · Docs on the world's chain in Device view; Full in a pane | Central, with AIKit | Docs stands the six tiers, the tier that needs me in front, each source with its readings: Recognised, Meaning, Moved since. Compact, a ladder of six rungs carries four ticks each: bound, recognised, holding, unmoved. | Reading changes nothing. A source that cannot be read shows the refusal word for word, and its row stays empty. | P |
| CD02 | [Arriving] A repository's account lies scattered across its documents, and I gather it tier by tier. | §18.5 Acts: Relate, Next · Docs, Tiers page | Central | From tier 0, I choose an unbound source and hold Relate, and Next brings up each empty tier in turn; each bound source keeps its bytes and its path, with its standing recorded. | Relate is held and returns a receipt. | P |
| CD03 | [Arriving] I make a vision document count as the world's ground. | §18.5 Acts: Recognise · Docs | Central | With Provenance and Standing set (Human-authored and Your position, for a vision of my own), holding Recognise records the source as ground; the document stays where it is, as it is. | Only a person recognises a source; an agent may propose one and never recognises it. | P |
| CD04 | [Yours] I check whether the code still says what a design says. | §18.5 What each row shows: Meaning · Docs | Central, with AIKit, reading the project's own law | Each mapping holds, or says how it does not: missing, the wrong relation, held twice, stale, or two faces bound as one; selecting a source shows the code it maps to. | Docs never writes the law between meaning and code and never guesses a mapping. | P |
| CD05 | [Yours] Before I hand a project to an agent, I see what moved since its account was last read. | §18.5 What each row shows: Moved since · Docs | Central | Moved since shows what changed since each source was last read against the work. | — | P |
| CD06 | [Yours] I want the readings fresh now. | §18.5 Acts: Read now; Refresh · Docs | Central | Read now reads each source against the code again; Refresh reads on open or only when I ask. | — | P |
| CD07 | [Arriving] A world has no account at all. | §18.5 Make page: Ensure · Docs | Central | Ensure gives the world its account; the Make page shows each part present or missing. | Nothing already there moves. | M |
| CD08 | [Yours] An old project's scaffold is incomplete. | §18.5 Make page: Reproject · Docs | Central | Reproject stamps the missing scaffold, with a receipt. | It moves, renames and deletes nothing. | M |
| CD09 | [Bare] My ground's root is missing parts. | §18.5 Make page: Initialise root · Docs | Central | Initialise root creates Control's user ground, governance, wiki and machines, the root's own state and Work where they are missing, with a receipt. | It keeps what is there. | M |
| CD10 | [Yours] I read what people wrote back against the experiences the project means to give. | §18.5 Returns page · Docs | Central | Each intended experience stands with the returns written against it. | Only a person binds a return. | P |
| CD11 | [Yours] A tier has no document yet, and I want to write one. | §18.5, §10.4 · Docs, a tier's row; the Document | Central | The Document opens from the tier's row to make the document. | The Document stays where I write; Docs holds the account. | P |
| CD12 | [Yours] I want Docs to show only what needs me, or to read one folder of a world. | §18.5 Parameters: Show, Subject · Docs | Central | Needs you hides the sources that are bound, recognised, holding and unmoved; Subject reads the world, or one folder or note in it. | — | P |
| CD13 | [Lived-in] I keep an eye on a world's account while I work in another mode. | §18.5 Widget · the Docs tile on the rail | Central | The tile shows the small ladder and how many sources wait; pressing a rung opens its tier. | — | P |
| CD14 | [Lived-in] I read a world's account as it stands on my other machines. | §18.5 Transport: Link, Follow · the transport | Central, with Workcell | Link reads the account on my other linked machines; Follow keeps Docs with the selected world, or off to hold its subject. | — | M |

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| DY02 | [Lived-in] I turn what I wrote on face 0 into today's tasks. | §18.2 Moves: Tasks · the Day's pads | Central (Day), with the Day's agent | The agent proposes tasks in three tiers, must, should and could, on face 1; I accept, re-tier or remove them. | The agent writes only as Scope and Arm allow: a proposal waits for me. | P |
| DY03 | [Lived-in] I work in any mode and need the day to keep track of what ran. | §18.2 The six faces, Where the faces come from · face 2 | Central (Day), reading every machine's NOW | Face 2 fills by itself as sessions start, run and finish: done, running and queued, each linked to where it runs. | — | H (core) |
| DY04 | [Lived-in] In the afternoon I want to see what keeps coming up. | §18.2 Moves: Patterns · the Day's pads | Central (Day), with the Day's agent | The agent proposes patterns on face 3, each with the items that show it; I keep or discard them. | — | P |
| DY05 | [Lived-in] I want the moments, people, places and files the day touched in one place. | §18.2 Moves: Context · the Day's pads | Central (Day), with the Day's agent | Face 4 gathers what the day's sessions touched, each entry with its author. | — | P |
| DY06 | [Lived-in] At the end of the day I write what it taught. | §18.2 Moves: Synthesis · the Day's pads, face 5 | Central (Day), with the Day's agent | The agent drafts what the day taught, what stays open and a proposal for tomorrow's face 0; I write beside it, each entry showing its author. | — | P |
| DY07 | [Lived-in] I make a move ask what I want of it. | §18.2 Pad setting: Prompt · the Day, Whole | Central (Day) | The pad's move asks the agent in my words from then on. | — | P |
| DY08 | [Lived-in] I check what a move last gave. | §18.2 Moves · a pad | Central (Day) | Each pad shows when it last ran and what it gave; clicking it opens what it proposed on its face. | — | P |
| DY09 | [Lived-in] I turn the die as the day moves. | §18.2 Parameters, Face; Transport, Tempo ‹ ›, Time signature · the Day; the transport | Central (Day) | The die turns to the face I chose, by Face, by Tempo one face at a time, by dragging it in Full, or by clicking a face; Time signature shows the four-and-two shape and the open face. | — | P |
| DY10 | [Lived-in] I want one face unfolded flat to write in. | §18.2 Parameters, View · the Day | Central (Day) | View shows the die, or one face flat. | — | P |
| DY11 | [Lived-in] I keep one mode's work off the day's faces. | §18.2 Parameters, Sources · the Day, Whole | Central (Day) | Sources set per mode which modes send entries to faces 2 and 4. | — | P |
| DY12 | [Lived-in] I glance at the day from any mode. | §18.2 Widget · the Day tile on the rail | Central (Day) | The tile shows today's die with open tasks, running sessions and new patterns; clicking a count opens that face in the pane that has focus. | — | P |
| DY13 | [Lived-in] I redesign the Day's page. | §18.2 Parameters, Template · the Day | Central (Day) | New Days are drawn by the template I chose. | Each Day keeps the template version it opened with; the template holds no logic, so a redesigned Day still turns, fills and closes. | P |

## CC — Let the day close and carry on tomorrow

**Why:** "the whole self-learning side of things, the whole self-improvement side of things" (UX chat 27 Oct).
**Where:** manual §5, §10.5, §18.1 (What carries to tomorrow), §18.2 (Two Days at a time, Close routine, Close-out) · **Port sources:** Obsidian 1.7.7 (saving)

| ID | Starting world and act | Where it happens | Owning operation and agent | Useful completion | Failure or continuation | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CC01 | [Lived-in] The day ends and I need it filed without setting any hours. | §5, §10.5, §18.2 Close routine · the Day | Central (Day) | The close runs by itself at the bounds Central keeps: it summarises the day, sends what I learned to my knowledge, and carries face 5's proposal to tomorrow's face 0. | The close shows on its pad when it last ran and keeps a record of every run. A run wanted at a set time is a routine in Settings, Automations. | P |
| CC02 | [Lived-in] I want the close to run now. | §18.2 Moves: Close; Compact, Close now; Transport, Loop · the Day; the transport | Central (Day) | The close routine runs at once. | — | P |
| CC03 | [Lived-in] I do not want the day closed for me. | §18.2 Parameters, Close routine · the Day | Central (Day) | With Close routine off, the day does not summarise or carry itself forward. | — | P |
| CC04 | [Lived-in] I need to know when the day opens, and when tomorrow's opens beside it. | §18.2 Opens tomorrow, Closes; Transport: Punch in, Punch out · the Day; the transport | Central (Day) | The bounds show as readings: Punch in shows when the day opened, Punch out when tomorrow's Day opens beside today's. | No hours are set on the device; Central keeps the day's bounds. | P |
| CC05 | [Lived-in] Tomorrow's Day has opened and I prepare it while today is still open. | §18.2 Two Days at a time · the Day full in a pane, its two tabs | Central (Day) | Today's and tomorrow's Days stand as two tabs; I write tomorrow's face 0. | When Central's day turns, tomorrow becomes today and the old Day closes. | P |
| CC06 | [Lived-in] I check that the last close left nothing behind. | §18.2 Whole, Close-out, Verify · the Day | Central (Day) | Close-out reads back the continuation registered, earlier handoffs closed, what was learned recorded and deferred work registered, and shows verified or how many clauses hold; pointing at it names those that fail. | Verify reads them again. | P |
| CC07 | [Lived-in] A page is still open at the close. | §18.1 What carries to tomorrow; §18.2 Where the faces come from · Session view, tomorrow's set | Central | It carries into tomorrow's set, playing, with today in its history, saying why it stays open and when to look at it again. | What I stopped leaves tomorrow's grid; today's Arrangement view is kept exactly as it was. | P |
| CC08 | [Lived-in] One of my machines was offline while it worked. | §18.2 Where the faces come from · faces 2 and 4 | Central, reading that machine's NOW | What it did arrives when it is back, at the time it happened. | A late result keeps the time it happened; the Day does not rewrite it as new. | M |
| CC09 | [Lived-in] I want to read an earlier day again. | §18.1 The set, §18.2 Rules · the browser, earlier Days | Central | The earlier Day opens as an earlier set, to read and walk through again. | A closed Day cannot be rewritten; a page carried forward is a new day's clip, with the old day in its history. | P |

## LV — Lay a piece out in parts and launch it

**Why:** "Just in the same way Ableton Live is professional software, it's not easy to use, but once you get your grips on it, it's incredibly fun because it opens up all these doors for creativity." (UX chat 27 Oct)
**Where:** manual §9, §21.1, §16.6, §10.5 (Live column) · **Port sources:** Ableton Live 12.0.25: session model, object model

| ID | Starting world and act | Where it happens | Owning operation and agent | Useful completion | Failure or continuation | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LV01 | [Bare] I open Live mode for the first time and want my set ready to play. | §21.1 · Session view, Device view, the rail · Torus and Trail tiles | Shell (Live): the set, opened in Live's own layout | Session view shows the set's parts, Device view the selected part's chain (note devices, the Torus, its effects), and the Torus tile already plays from its short keyboard | While the set loads, the parts it last showed stand at once and slots still being read are dimmed; if the set's owner cannot be reached, nothing launches (§10.2) | M |
| LV02 | [Bare] I drag the Torus from the browser onto an empty track to start a part I can play. | §10.3, §16.7 Start a track, §21.1 · the browser (Instruments) · Session view | Shell (Live): the set; Quaternal Logic: the Torus at the head of the part's chain | A MIDI part stands with the Torus on its chain, and its notes play the Torus | The torus is the only generator: a part's sound always comes from it, and an audio part plays samples instead (§21.1, §21.2 Rules) | M |
| LV03 | [Lived-in] I add an audio part to play recorded sound beside the torus. | §21.1 The track, The input · Session view | Shell (Live): the set | The audio part plays its samples and takes its input from my audio interface, as a Live audio track does | An act the set cannot take stays dimmed in the part's menu, and the status bar names the operation it waits for (§10.9) | M |
| LV04 | [Lived-in] I group several parts so they fold under one head. | §21.1, §16.6 The track, §10.8 · Session view · ⌘G, U | Shell (Live): the set | The group's head folds its members; a row launched in a group launches all its children | Undo takes the grouping back as one named step (§10.9) | P |
| LV05 | [Lived-in] I switch a part off, solo another and set level and pan while the piece plays. | §21.1 The mixer row, §16.6 · Session view, mixer (⌥⌘M) · Activator, Solo, Volume and pan | Shell (Live): the set | Off, the part makes no sound and keeps its place and settings; Solo lets me hear only the soloed parts while the others keep running | Solo and Arm wait in the track's menu, because S plays a key of the Jankó keyboard (§10.9) | M |
| LV06 | [Lived-in] I send some of a part to a return track and choose where its output goes. | §21.1 The mixer row · mixer, sends (⌥⌘S), return tracks (⌥⌘R) · Sends, Output | Shell (Live): the set | The return's chain works on what several parts send it; a part's output goes to the Main track, a group or another part | Output defaults to the Main track, whose chain works on everything I hear (§21.1 The Main track) | M |
| LV07 | [Lived-in] I set a part's Monitor so I hear my playing through its chain only while it is armed. | §21.1 The mixer row · mixer, In/Out (⌥⌘I) · Monitor | Shell (Live): the set | Auto passes my playing through the chain while armed; In always passes it; Off passes only the clips | Disarmed under Auto, my playing no longer reaches the chain and the clips still play | M |
| LV08 | [Lived-in] I launch clips in Session view to try parts against each other. | §16.6 Session view, §10.2 · Session view · a clip's ▶, Enter | Shell (Live): the set | Each clip shows its state (waiting, running, stopped, recording) and the part plays it; a slot's stop square stops the part | A deactivated clip is grey and does not launch (§10.2) | M |
| LV09 | [Lived-in] I launch a row across all the parts, with the row's own tempo and signature. | §21.1 The Main track · Session view · a row's launch button, 1 to 9 | Shell (Live): the set | Every clip in the row starts together, at the row's tempo and signature | A row launches only what is in it; Stop pressed twice cancels at once and launch quantize does not delay it (§16.6 Rules) | M |
| LV10 | [Lived-in] I stop everything I launched, then go back to the arrangement. | §21.1 The Main track · Session view · Stop All Clips, Back to Arrangement | Shell (Live): the set | Every part stops; Back to Arrangement, lit while the Session differs from the Arrangement, returns playback to the ruler | The clips I launched stay in their slots for the next try | M |
| LV11 | [Lived-in] I set what follows a clip so the piece runs on without my pressing each one. | §21.1 The Launch panel, The Main track · Clip view (Launch panel); the Main track's follow-action switch | Shell (Live): the set | Action A and B with their Chance, Linked to a number of loops or Unlinked to a time, Jump to a clip I choose; the Main track's switch turns follow actions on for the whole set | A clip laid on Arrangement view's ruler is not launched and its Launch panel is empty (§21.1) | M |
| LV12 | [Lived-in] I choose how a clip launches and when its launch lands. | §21.1 The Launch panel · Clip view · Launch mode, Legato, Quantize, Velocity | Shell (Live): the clip | Trigger plays on a press, Gate while held, Toggle on alternate presses, Repeat retriggers at the quantize rate; Legato takes over the play position of the clip it replaces | Quantize on Global follows the transport's Quantize (§21.1) | M |
| LV13 | [Bare] I set the tempo, tap it in, set the metre and the click, and when launches land. | §10.5 (Live), §21.2 Transport · the transport · Tap, Tempo, Time signature, Metronome, Quantize | Shell (Live): the set | The whole set plays at that tempo and metre, with the click on the beat and launches landing on the quantize I chose | A row that carries its own tempo and signature takes over when it is launched (§21.1) | M |
| LV14 | [Lived-in] I share the set's tempo with other players. | §10.5 (Live) · the transport · Link | Shell (Live) | My set keeps time with the other players | The control keeps its place in every mode and means that mode's link elsewhere (§10.5) | M |
| LV15 | [Lived-in] I read the sample rate, the processor load and where the playhead is while I play. | §10.5 (Live), §10.1 · the transport · Rate, Load, Follow, Position | Shell (Live) | Rate shows the sample rate, Load the processor load, Position bars, beats and sixteenths; Follow keeps the view with the playhead | A reading whose state is not known stays dark (§10.4) | M |

## LT — Play the torus

**Why:** "The Taurus, the music mode in Live, and the Taurus, the M1 Taurus, is completely based on quaternal logic. It genuinely is the form of quaternal logic." (UX chat 27 Oct)
**Where:** manual §9, §21.1 The input, §21.2, §16.15 Torus · **Port sources:** Ableton Live 12.0.25: object model, device decodes

| ID | Starting world and act | Where it happens | Owning operation and agent | Useful completion | Failure or continuation | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LT01 | [Bare] I play a chord on the Jankó keyboard under the torus and see its shape. | §9, §21.2 The keyboard, Full · Pane view · Torus (Full) with its Jankó keyboard | Quaternal Logic: the Torus (M1′) strikes each key's point on its helix and sounds it; pointer, touch, computer keys and MIDI share one path | Several points light together and the surface draws the chord's figure, the shape of the symbol it belongs to; the same fingering at another key moves the figure along the helix unchanged | Releasing a key releases the note while the body rings on for Release; losing focus or a device releases every held note; Panic releases all at once | H (core) |
| LT02 | [Bare] I touch the torus's surface to hear the body ring where I touch. | §9, §21.2 · Pane view · Torus (Full), the surface | Quaternal Logic: the Torus | The body rings at the point I touch; touching while a chord sounds plays the surface by hand | A camera move does not change the sound unless Receiver is On (§21.2 Rules) | M |
| LT03 | [Bare] I play the part from the computer keys. | §21.1 The input, §10.8 · Torus (Full), the playing surface · Input: Computer keys | Shell (Live): the playing surface; Quaternal Logic: the Torus | The keys strike points on the helices through the same path as the pointer | Keys never play notes while I type in a document, and a key held while I type in a field does not play (§10.8, §21.2) | M |
| LT04 | [Lived-in] I play from my MIDI controller and map its controls to the torus. | §21.1 The input, §21.2 Transport · the playing surface · Input: MIDI; Keys and maps, MIDI map | Shell (Live): the input path; Quaternal Logic: the Torus | The controller's keys strike the same points; its mapped knobs move the torus's parameters | An input set Off is ignored; if the device is lost, every note it held is released (§21.1 Rules) | M |
| LT05 | [Bare] I switch to an ordinary piano layout because I would rather play that way. | §9, §21.1 The input · the playing surface · Layout: Piano | Shell (Live): the playing surface | The piano plays the same points on the torus, and the roll's key column shows the same layout | Either layout strikes points whose places come from the torus's geometry, never set by eye (§21.2 Rules) | M |
| LT06 | [Lived-in] I widen the playing surface and transpose every key without changing my fingering. | §21.1 The input · the playing surface · Range, Transpose | Shell (Live): the playing surface | The surface shows 2 to 8 octaves; transposed, every chord keeps its shape under my hand | The keyboard shows its range and recolours to the mode in force (§21.2 The keyboard) | M |
| LT07 | [Lived-in] I choose where a note's strength comes from and let pressure on a held key move the sound. | §21.1 The input · the playing surface · Velocity, Pressure | Shell (Live): the playing surface; Quaternal Logic: the Torus | Pointer height, pressure or a fixed value sets how hard each point is struck; pressure on a held key moves the sound and records into the clip's MPE tab | Pressure or bend on a held note moves the sound and never changes the tuning (§21.4 Rules) | M |
| LT08 | [Lived-in] I play with one hand using Sustain, and choose whether two keys of one pitch hold one note or a note each. | §21.1 The input · the playing surface · Sustain, Repeat touch | Shell (Live): the playing surface; Quaternal Logic: the Torus | Sustain holds notes after I lift; Each key makes every key its own touch point | A held note keeps its own tail: a key release does not cut the body's ringing (§21.2 Rules) | M |
| LT09 | [Lived-in] I play more notes than the torus allows and stop them all at once. | §21.2 Parameters, The keyboard · Torus (Whole) · Voices; Panic | Quaternal Logic: the Torus | The oldest note makes way for the new one; Panic stops every note at once | Voices runs from 1 to 32 and holds the limit I set | M |
| LT10 | [Lived-in] I keep the Torus tile on the rail and play its short keyboard while I work elsewhere. | §21.1 On the rail, §21.2 Widget, §10.6 · the rail · Torus tile | Shell (Live): the tile; Quaternal Logic: the Torus | The small torus shows its level and the notes that sound; clicking it opens the torus full in the pane that has focus | The tile stays on the rail when I change mode, like every widget (§10.6) | M |
| LT11 | [Lived-in] I pop the torus out into a window of its own to perform from a full screen. | §16.15 Torus, §10.4 · the device head's ↗ · Torus (Pop-out) | Shell (Live) | The torus and its keyboard fill the screen, with the controls in one row under them | Every view shows the same live settings, so a change in the window shows on the chain (§10.4) | M |
| LT12 | [Lived-in] I change what the pane draws and make my camera the place where the torus is heard. | §21.2 Parameters · Torus (Whole, Full) · View, Receiver | Quaternal Logic: the Torus | Surface, Wire or Both is drawn; with Receiver On, moving the camera moves where I hear from | With Receiver Off a camera move changes nothing in the sound (§21.2 Rules) | M |
| LT13 | [Lived-in] I read on the playing surface whether a point was struck by me, previewed as a route or played by a generated walk. | §21.1 Rules · the playing surface | Shell (Live): the playing surface | The three look different, so I know what struck each point | The surface draws no relation between neighbouring keys (§21.1 Rules) | P |

## LB — Shape the torus's sound and keep it

**Why:** "Musicians would excel in the live portion, in the M1 torus and M2 surfaces, so literal synth and sound design." (UX chat 27 Oct)
**Where:** manual §9, §21.2 Parameters, The body source, The stage, Views; §16.15 Torus · **Port sources:** Ableton Live 12.0.25: device decodes

| ID | Starting world and act | Where it happens | Owning operation and agent | Useful completion | Failure or continuation | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LB01 | [Lived-in] I move Pulse mix off the middle to make the two pulses beat. | §21.2 Parameters, §16.15 · Device view · Torus (Compact, handle 1 on the pulse line; Whole, Pulses) | Quaternal Logic: the Torus (M1′, Spanda) | 0% sounds the three-and-three pulse alone, 100% the four-and-two alone; between them I hear them beat | With Arm on, the move is recorded as an automation lane (§21.2 Transport) | M |
| LB02 | [Lived-in] I balance the two helices and drive the body harder. | §21.2 Parameters, §16.15 · Torus (Compact, handle 2 out from the ring; Whole) · Helix balance, Drive | Quaternal Logic: the Torus | The sound leans to one helix or the other; Drive shakes the body harder | When the sky moves Drive, its modulation arc shows it (§16.15) | M |
| LB03 | [Lived-in] I raise Damping and Release for a long bell tone, or lower them for a dry, short one. | §21.2 Parameters, Tips · Torus (Compact macros) · Damping, Release | Quaternal Logic: the Torus | The body rings longer and each note fades slower, or both shorten | A tail goes on after I release a key (§21.5 Rules) | M |
| LB04 | [Lived-in] I set how hard velocity strikes the body, how many of its modes take part and how much its surface is kicked about. | §21.2 Parameters · Torus (Whole, Body) · Strike, Modes, Agitation | Quaternal Logic: the Torus | The body answers harder or softer, fuller or thinner, calmer or rougher | Under Body source Tattva these values hold the tattva's and do not move (LB05) | M |
| LB05 | [Lived-in] I let the tattva of the M2 condition set the body. | §21.2 The body source · Torus (Whole, Body) · Body source: Tattva | Quaternal Logic: the Torus, with M2′'s tattvas | Strike, Damping and Agitation show and hold the values of the condition's element: Earth, Water, Fire or Air | Under Space the body keeps my values; to set them by hand I set Body source back to Declared | M |
| LB06 | [Lived-in] I set how fast a note rises and where the part sits in level and pan. | §21.2 Parameters · Torus (Whole, Envelope; Voices and output) · Attack, Level and pan | Quaternal Logic: the Torus | Notes swell in or strike at once; the part sits louder, softer, left or right | Release, not Attack, sets how long the body rings after a key is lifted | M |
| LB07 | [Lived-in] I save the torus's state as a preset so I can come back to this sound. | §9, §21.2 To use, Rules · the device head; the browser (the Torus's saved sounds) | Shell (Live): the Torus's presets in the browser | The preset stands under the Torus in the browser and opens with the same sound, because it keeps every parameter | A private context does not enter a shared preset (§22.6 Rules) | M |
| LB08 | [Lived-in] I start a new part from a saved sound. | §16.7 Start a track · the browser · a Torus preset dragged onto an empty track | Shell (Live): the set | A part starts with that sound on its Torus | Dragged into a pane instead, the preset opens there and starts no track (§10.3) | M |
| LB09 | [Lived-in] I evaluate a fold procedure once so the form folds a set way. | §21.2 The stage · Torus (Whole, Stage page) · Procedure, Evaluate | Quaternal Logic: the Torus's stage | The fold owns the form slot; its receipt comes back with the field as it stands, and its passage runs the scene it stands and the two that follow | A procedure that runs when evaluated binds to nothing | P |
| LB10 | [Lived-in] I bind a strike to the ring so the torus is struck at a planet's mode at every step. | §21.2 The stage · Stage page · Bind, Budget | Quaternal Logic: the Torus's stage | The strike fires at each step of the ring, sixteen times by default, and the page counts the firings spent | When its budget is spent it stops and says so; a strike claims no slot and survives no new evaluation | M |
| LB11 | [Lived-in] I unbind a procedure from the live flow. | §21.2 The stage · Stage page · Unbind | Quaternal Logic: the Torus's stage | The binding is removed and the page names the standing it ends in | Unbinding keeps the slots it holds; Retire releases them (LB12) | P |
| LB12 | [Lived-in] I retire a procedure to give its slots back. | §21.2 The stage · Stage page · Retire | Quaternal Logic: the Torus's stage | The slots return to their authored state, and what the procedure left stays as authored material | A freed slot can be owned by the next procedure I evaluate (LB13) | P |
| LB13 | [Lived-in] I try to evaluate a second procedure that writes a slot another already owns. | §21.2 The stage, Rules · Stage page · Evaluate | Quaternal Logic: the Torus's stage | I learn the slot is held and free it by retiring the first procedure | The second write is refused until the first retires, because the order in which two writes arrive decides nothing | P |

## LM — Play in a mode and tune in exact ratios

**Why:** "So Quaternal logic, as such, expresses one-to-one with the music theory." (UX chat 27 Oct)
**Where:** manual §9, §21.1 The Clip panel and Editor tabs, §21.3, §21.4 · **Port sources:** Ableton Live 12.0.25: session model, device decodes

| ID | Starting world and act | Where it happens | Owning operation and agent | Useful completion | Failure or continuation | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LM01 | [Bare] I set the mode in force in the transport so the whole set plays in it. | §9, §10.5, §21.2 Transport, §21.3 The modes · the transport · Key, root and scale | Shell (Live): the set's mode in force | One of twelve roots, one for each lens, and one of seven modes, with the tuning, hold for the whole set; the keyboard recolours and the roll follows | A lens, a root and a mode name are not interchangeable: the 84 modes are 12 roots times 7 modes (§21.3 Rules) | M |
| LM02 | [Lived-in] I give one clip a mode of its own so the piece moves from mode to mode phrase by phrase. | §21.1 The Clip panel · Clip view · Scale | Shell (Live): the clip | The next phrase plays in the clip's mode, and Scale mapping on the chain follows it | Scale Off returns the clip to the set's mode in force; audio clips carry no mode | M |
| LM03 | [Lived-in] I play a phrase in a source scale with its own intervals. | §9, §21.1 The Clip panel, §21.3 · Clip view or Scale mapping · Source scale; Family: Source scale | Shell (Live): the clip and the part's chain | A maqām keeps its own intervals, its tonic and its dominant, and is not turned into an equal scale | The Ficino planetary scales and the Shem names are named and not yet served; a maqām has no set of the twelve notes, so Scale mapping passes notes unchanged (§21.3) | M |
| LM04 | [Lived-in] I put Scale mapping before the torus so whatever keys I press land on the mode. | §21.3 · Device view, the part's chain before the Torus · Scale mapping | Shell (Live): the part's chain | A note outside the mode moves to its nearest note in the mode; a note in the mode passes unchanged; Tie decides a note an equal way from both | Off passes every note as I play it; the device changes which note plays and never sets the tuning (§21.3 Rules) | M |
| LM05 | [Lived-in] I let the sky set the mode, so the hour's planet chooses a maqām. | §21.3 Parameters, §22.1 The day and the hour · Scale mapping (Whole) · Set by: Sky | Shell (Live): the part's chain; Quaternal Logic: the Sky reads the planet of the hour | Root, Mode name and Source scale show the Sky's choice and hold it; a phrase played across the hour moves to the next planet's maqām | When the Sky has no place to count the hours from, Sky stands closed | M |
| LM06 | [Lived-in] I address the lens mode in fifths rather than chromatically. | §21.3 Parameters, §21.1 Editor tabs · Scale mapping; the key column · Basis | Shell (Live): the part's chain | The lens mode is addressed in the order of fifths; the key column's own Basis sets the order its overlay uses | Basis changes the order the twelve notes are addressed in, not their tuning | M |
| LM07 | [Lived-in] I fold the roll to the clip's mode, or highlight it in place. | §21.1 Editor tabs · Clip view, Notes tab · Fold, Fold to Scale, Highlight Scale | Shell (Live): the clip's editor | Only the rows that hold notes, or only the mode's rows, show; or the mode's rows are tinted | A mode folds or highlights the keys and rows and leaves the notes as they are (§21.1 Rules) | P |
| LM08 | [Lived-in] I change what the key colours show: the mode, a traditional keyboard's black and white, the pair family or the completion degrees. | §21.1 Editor tabs · Clip view, Notes tab key column · Overlay | Shell (Live): the key column | Partition shows the two helices as black and white; Pair family marks A, B and C; Completion marks D1 to D3 | Changing the overlay does not change the tuning (§21.1 Rules) | P |
| LM09 | [Lived-in] I move a clip's notes onto its mode. | §21.1 Tools (Pitch and time) · Clip view · Fit to Scale | Shell (Live): the clip | The notes move onto the clip's mode | Undo takes it back as one named step (§10.9) | M |
| LM10 | [Lived-in] I put Exact-ratio tuning before the torus and hear a fifth at exactly 3:2. | §21.4 · the part's chain before the Torus · Exact-ratio tuning: Reference note, Reference frequency, Ratio set | Shell (Live): the part's chain | Every note's pitch is an exact ratio from the reference; Fifths stack builds them from 3:2 | Only Equal rounds to the equal scale; under Chromatic stack the six notes of the other whole-tone family have no ratio until their step is stated | M |
| LM11 | [Lived-in] I read each note's ratio, cents or hertz and how far it lies from the equal scale. | §21.4 Parameters, Full · Exact-ratio tuning (Full: the ratios on the helices) · Readout, Error | Shell (Live): the part's chain | Playing a chord shows each note's ratio and its error in cents | MIDI note number, exact frequency and a vibration mode's pitch stay three different values (§21.4 Rules) | M |
| LM12 | [Lived-in] I keep the comma open to hear the gap twelve fifths leave, or fold it into the octave. | §21.4 Parameters · Exact-ratio tuning · Comma | Shell (Live): the part's chain | Keep open shows and sounds the gap; Fold into octave closes the circle of fifths | The device never hides the gap unless I fold it (§21.4 Rules) | M |
| LM13 | [Lived-in] I tune to the planets' just intervals. | §21.4 Parameters · Exact-ratio tuning · Ratio set: Source ratios | Shell (Live): the part's chain, with M2′'s intervals | Sun 1:1, Moon 4:3, Mercury 5:4, Venus 9:8, Mars 15:8, Jupiter 5:3, Saturn 3:2, Neptune 9:5, Pluto 9:4 | Uranus has no interval (§22.2) | M |
| LM14 | [Lived-in] I send the tuning to a hardware synth. | §21.4 Parameters, Tips · Exact-ratio tuning (Whole, Export) · Export: MPE, Bend range 48 | Shell (Live): the part's chain | The synth plays the exact ratios through each note's bend | Each export keeps the intended ratio, the rule of approximation and the error (§21.4 Rules) | M |
| LM15 | [Lived-in] I open Scale mapping full and watch a key outside the mode move onto it. | §21.3 Full · Pane view · Scale mapping (Full) | Shell (Live) | The mode's notes show on the two helices and the others stay dim | A mode of a scale is not a mode of vibration of the plate (§21.3 Rules) | P |

## LE — Shape the sound with effects

**Why:** "the system is built around this plug-and-play devices and instruments ontology from Ableton" (UX chat 27 Oct)
**Where:** manual §9, §21.5, §10.4 · **Port sources:** Ableton Live 12.0.25: device decodes

| ID | Starting world and act | Where it happens | Owning operation and agent | Useful completion | Failure or continuation | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LE01 | [Lived-in] I add an effect after the torus by pressing its name, and take it off by pressing again. | §21.5 · Device view, the part's chain after the Torus · Filter, Dynamics, Delay, Reverb | Shell (Live): the part's chain | The effect goes on at the end of the chain with its own light; taken off, it keeps its settings for when it returns | With none on the chain the torus sounds as it is | M |
| LE02 | [Lived-in] I move an effect earlier and hear the order change the sound. | §21.5, Rules · the part's chain · ◂ on an effect | Shell (Live): the part's chain | Dynamics before a Delay lowers the sound and its repeats apart; after it, together, so the repeats come out quieter | A Filter, a Delay and a Reverb on their own sound the same in any order | M |
| LE03 | [Lived-in] I cut or lift a range of the torus's frequencies with the Filter. | §21.5 Filter · Filter (Compact, Whole: the response display) · Type, Cutoff, Resonance, Drive, Mix | Shell (Live): the part's chain | Dragging the curve's handle across moves the cutoff and up raises the resonance; the second handle sets the drive | The light shows the filter on only when it reports so (§10.4) | M |
| LE04 | [Lived-in] I lower the loud parts of the torus and lift the whole with Dynamics. | §21.5 Dynamics · Dynamics (Whole: curve, time display, Level and Time pads) · Threshold, Ratio, Attack, Release, Makeup | Shell (Live): the part's chain | The curve shows level out for each level in; the time display shows how far a burst is lowered and how it lets go; gain reduction reads now | Placed after a Delay it lowers the repeats with the sound (LE02) | M |
| LE05 | [Lived-in] I repeat the torus after a time with the Delay, in step with the set. | §21.5 Delay · Delay (Whole: the repeats) · Sync, Note length, Time, Feedback, Tone, Mix | Shell (Live): the part's chain | With Sync on the time is a note length at the set's tempo; the display draws each repeat's level and bright part | With Sync on the handles move only up and down, because the note length holds the time | M |
| LE06 | [Lived-in] I give the torus a room to sound in with the Reverb. | §21.5 Reverb · Reverb (Whole: the tail, Room and Tone pads) · Size, Decay, Damping, Pre-delay, Dry/wet | Shell (Live): the part's chain | The display draws the gap, the first reflections and the tail falling away | To keep a dry take I leave the Reverb off while I record (§21.5 Tips) | M |
| LE07 | [Lived-in] I keep an effect's tile on the rail and switch it from there. | §21.5 Widget views, §10.6 · the rail · an effect's tile | Shell (Live) | The tile shows the effect's display and main readings; clicking its light turns it on or off | Its settings stay live whether or not the tile shows (§10.6) | M |
| LE08 | [Lived-in] I keep the chain with my set so its effects, order and settings come back. | §9, §21.5 Rules, §10.2 What is kept · Device view | Shell (Live): the set keeps its tracks, clips and settings | Reopening the set brings back each part's chain as I left it | Which device stands expanded is kept by the workspace, not the set (§16.5) | M |

## LN — Edit notes and samples in Clip view
